# Deep Code Review: Desloppify

**11 specialized reviewers examined every file from 7 different angles. Here are the consolidated findings, deduplicated and ranked.**

---

## CRITICAL / Must Fix

### 1. Signature variance is completely dead for both Python AND TypeScript
**Files:** `lang/python/extractors.py:83`, `lang/typescript/extractors.py:67`

Neither `extract_py_functions` nor `extract_ts_functions` populates the `params` field on `FunctionInfo`. It defaults to `[]`, so `detect_signature_variance` in `signature.py` always sees empty param lists, and the `len(param_signatures) < 2` guard is always true. **The entire signature variance detector silently produces zero results for both languages.**

### 2. `async def` functions invisible to Python extractor
**File:** `lang/python/extractors.py:55`

The regex `^(\s*)def\s+(\w+)\s*\(` never matches `async def`. In async-heavy codebases (FastAPI, aiohttp), a large fraction of functions are invisible to the duplicate detector, passthrough detector, and function extractor.

### 3. TS function extractor regex has catastrophic backtracking risk
**File:** `lang/typescript/extractors.py:28-34`

The fragment `(?:\([^)]*\)|[^=])*=>` in `fn_re` creates nested alternations with near-universal character classes. On long lines with complex type annotations that don't end in `=>`, this can cause exponential backtracking, potentially hanging the tool.

### 4. `move` command has zero error handling — partial writes corrupt code
**File:** `commands/move.py:136-157`

`_apply_changes` does `shutil.move` first, then updates importers in a loop with bare `write_text`. If any write fails (permissions, disk full), the file has been moved but only some imports updated. **No rollback, no backup, no error handling.** The codebase is left in a broken state.

### 5. All fixers use non-atomic file writes on user source code
**Files:** `fixers/common.py:137`, `fixers/logs.py:71`, `fixers/params.py:85`, `fixers/vars.py:109`

State writes use atomic temp+rename pattern, but every fixer writes user source code with bare `write_text()`. A crash mid-write can corrupt source files with no backup.

### 6. TS `subprocess.run` for `tsc` has no timeout — can hang forever
**File:** `lang/typescript/detectors/unused.py:30-33`

`npx tsc` has no `timeout` parameter. If tsc stalls (prompting to install, massive codebase), the CLI blocks indefinitely. The Python equivalents (`ruff`, `pyflakes`) correctly use `timeout=60`.

---

## HIGH Severity

### 7. `compute_narrative` mutates state (violates purity contract)
**File:** `narrative.py:785-786`

The module docstring says "Pure functions… no side effects" but `_compute_reminders` writes to `state["reminder_history"]`. Calling `compute_narrative` twice per cycle double-increments reminder counters.

### 8. `lru_cache` on function reading mutable global state
**File:** `utils.py:217-241`

`_find_source_files_cached` reads `_extra_exclusions` (a mutable global) but that value is NOT part of the cache key. Cache correctness depends entirely on `set_exclusions` calling `cache_clear()` — a fragile coupling.

### 9. Zone suffix pattern uses `in` instead of `endswith` 
**File:** `zones.py:59-60`

Pattern `.test` matches via `pattern in basename` (substring). So `.test` matches `file.testing.py`, `file.test_helpers.py`, etc. The docstring says "suffix/extension" (implies endswith). The `_` prefix handler on line 68 correctly uses `endswith`.

### 10. Comment-stripping regex corrupts strings containing `//`
**Files:** `smells.py:601-602`, `react.py:75-76`, multiple helpers

`re.sub(r"//[^\n]*", "", body)` strips `//` comments but also strips URLs inside strings (`"https://example.com"` → `"https:`). Used to determine if blocks are "empty" — can cause false positives/negatives.

### 11. `catch_re.finditer(content)` has no string/comment awareness
**File:** `lang/typescript/detectors/smells.py:620-621, 881-882`

The catch regex is applied to full file content with no string/template literal filtering. A `catch` inside a template literal or test fixture string would create a false match, extracting nonsensical "body" from subsequent real code.

### 12. `confidence="info"` undefined in scoring system
**File:** `commands/scan.py:43`

`_audit_excluded_dirs` creates findings with `confidence="info"`, but this value doesn't exist in `CONFIDENCE_WEIGHTS` or `CONFIDENCE_ORDER`. Falls back to default weight 0.7, silently miscalculating scores.

### 13. Params fixer `count=1` substitution can break code
**File:** `fixers/params.py:72`

`re.sub(..., count=1)` renames only the first occurrence. For `function process(data, metadata = data.length)`, renaming `data` → `_data` only renames the parameter, leaving `data.length` referencing a nonexistent name. **This breaks working code.**

### 14. `_is_param_context` unconditionally returns True at line 114
**File:** `fixers/params.py:114`

After the for-loop checking for function/catch context, the function has an unconditional `return True`, meaning ANY code inside parentheses is treated as a parameter context. This could cause the fixer to rename non-parameter variables.

---

## MEDIUM Severity

### 15. Duplicate `TIER_WEIGHTS` and `_EXCLUDED_ZONES` definitions
**Files:** `state.py:120` + `scoring.py:34`, `scoring.py:46` + `zones.py:25,28`

Two copies of `TIER_WEIGHTS` and two definitions of excluded zones. `zones.py` already exports `EXCLUDED_ZONE_VALUES` for exactly this purpose but `scoring.py` doesn't use it.

### 16. `_detect_phase` returns "early_momentum" for declining trajectories
**File:** `narrative.py:151-157`

If `len(history)` is 2-5 and the overall score went DOWN (but the most recent scan ticked up slightly), the code falls through to `return "early_momentum"`. A project sliding backwards gets optimistic framing.

### 17. `PROJECT_ROOT` computed at import time from `cwd()`
**File:** `utils.py:10`

Module-level constant computed at import time. Wrong if imported before the working directory is set, or when used as a library from a different directory. Makes testing difficult.

### 18. Python class regex doesn't handle multi-line base class lists
**File:** `lang/python/extractors.py:146`

`class MyView(\n    LoginRequiredMixin,\n    DetailView,\n):` is not captured. These are often the most complex classes (multiple inheritance).

### 19. `[^)]*` with `re.DOTALL` breaks on nested parentheses
**Files:** `lang/python/detectors/complexity.py:11`, `lang/python/extractors.py:249`

For `def foo(bar: Callable[..., tuple[int, ...]])`, the `[^)]*` stops at the first `)` inside the type annotation. Causes incorrect parameter counting and function boundary detection.

### 20. Naming detector misclassifies `snake_case` as `camelCase`
**File:** `detectors/naming.py:22-23`

`stem.islower() and "-" not in stem` catches `my_utils` and labels it `"camelCase"`. All-lowercase filenames with underscores get the wrong label.

### 21. `_detect_catch_return_default` doesn't receive pre-split `lines`
**File:** `lang/typescript/detectors/smells.py:950`

Calls `content.splitlines()` on every catch block match, unlike every other helper which receives `lines` as a parameter. Wasteful and inconsistent.

### 22. `custom_hooks` count double-counts built-in hooks
**File:** `lang/typescript/extractors.py:134`

`use[A-Z]\w+\s*\(` matches `useEffect`, `useState`, `useRef` etc., inflating `custom_hooks`. The god component rule threshold fires more easily than intended.

### 23. Hardcoded `src/tools/` path in coupling output and `get_area`
**Files:** `detectors/coupling.py:89`, `utils.py:294-307`

Project-specific path assumptions baked into generic utility code.

### 24. `except\s*(?:\w|:)` misses tuple except clauses  
**File:** `lang/python/detectors/smells.py:215`

`except (TypeError, ValueError) as e:` won't match because `(` isn't `\w` or `:`. These except blocks are silently skipped by `_walk_except_blocks`.

### 25. `_smell_helpers.py` is a complete duplicate of code in `smells.py`
**File:** `lang/typescript/detectors/_smell_helpers.py`

Contains all the same multi-line detector functions already in `smells.py`. Bug fixes to one may miss the other.

### 26. Schema drift clustering uses seed-only comparison (not single-linkage)
**File:** `lang/python/detectors/dict_keys.py:552-567`

The inner loop always compares against `lit["keys"]` (the seed) rather than each cluster member. Items similar to non-seed members but not to the seed won't join the cluster.

### 27. `_write_query` and `compute_tool_hash` have no error handling
**Files:** `cli.py:14-22`, `utils.py:269-279`

Both can crash the entire command at the tail end of a successful scan. `_write_query` uses `default=str` (produces garbled `PosixPath(...)` for Path objects) while `save_state` uses `_json_default`.

### 28. `load_state` doesn't catch `OSError`
**File:** `state.py:45-46`

Catches `JSONDecodeError` and `UnicodeDecodeError` but not permission/filesystem errors. A `chmod 000` state file crashes with unhandled `PermissionError`.

### 29. `_count_return_fields` depth check timing bug
**File:** `lang/typescript/detectors/react.py:251-273`

Depth is checked after processing the line's braces, not before. A `return {` line that changes depth from 1→2 would fail the `depth == 1` check.

---

## LOW Severity (selected highlights)

- **`_finding_in_dimension` has unused `dim_scores` parameter** (narrative.py:240)
- **`_compute_badge_status` does filesystem I/O in a "pure" module** (narrative.py:614)
- **Auto-resolving `wontfix` findings** when they disappear from scans (state.py:264)
- **`make_finding` doesn't validate tier or confidence values** (state.py:216)
- **`_upsert_findings` selective field update** is fragile for future fields (state.py:308)
- **`FileZoneMap.get` returns PRODUCTION for unknown paths** (zones.py:130)
- **Double AST parsing** in Python smells + dict_keys detectors per file
- **`_READ_METHODS` / `_WRITE_METHODS` dead code** in dict_keys.py:34-35
- **`detect_context_nesting` processes closing tags before opening tags** on same-line providers (react.py:139)
- **Escaped backslash handling** (`prev_ch != "\\"`) in all brace trackers mishandles `\\"`
- **`DET_ORDER` and `DETECTOR_NAMES` hardcoded lists** are out of sync with actual detectors
- **`extract_functions` type annotation says `list[dict]` but returns `list[FunctionInfo]`** (base.py:76)
- **Visualize module XSS-adjacent**: `</script>` in filenames could break HTML output (visualize.py:121)
- **Hardcoded "src" root assumption** in visualize tree builder (visualize.py:45)
- **`_DEBUG_COMMENT_RE` too broad** — could delete legitimate comments containing "log" or "debug" (fixers/logs.py:80)

---

## Systemic / Architectural Issues

1. **Zero automated tests** — The biggest risk. ~4,500 lines of detectors, scoring, narrative, and state management with no test suite. Any refactoring is flying blind.

2. **Global mutable state everywhere** — `_extra_exclusions`, `PROJECT_ROOT`, `lang._zone_map`, `_registry`. Makes the code untestable, non-reentrant, and unsuitable for library use.

3. **Untyped dict-based data model** — Findings, state, potentials, actions are all `dict` with no schema enforcement. A `TypedDict` or dataclass layer would catch many of the schema drift issues found above.

4. **CLI layer leakage** — Private `_state_path`, `_write_query`, `_resolve_lang` from `cli.py` are imported by every command. `sys.exit(1)` in utility functions prevents library use.

5. **`stale_exclude` detector orphaned** — Not in any scoring Dimension or DETECTOR_TOOLS. Findings are stored but contribute nothing to objective scores and get no narrative treatment.

---

**Total unique issues identified across all reviewers: ~75+, with 6 critical, 8 high, 15 medium, and many low-severity findings.** The most impactful fixes would be: populating `params` in both extractors, fixing the catastrophic backtracking regex, adding error handling to `move`/fixers, and adding a timeout to the `tsc` subprocess call.

# Deep Code Review: Cross-Language Detector Framework

## Files Reviewed

- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors/__init__.py`
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors/base.py`
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors/complexity.py`
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors/coupling.py`
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors/dupes.py`
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors/facade.py`
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors/flat_dirs.py`

- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors/passthrough.py`
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors/signature.py`
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors/single_use.py`

---

## 1. Bugs and Logic Errors

### 1a. Naming detector: `snake_case` files classified as `camelCase` (BUG)

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors/naming.py`, lines 15-23

```python
if "-" in stem and stem == stem.lower():
    return "kebab-case"
if stem[0].isupper() and "-" not in stem:
    return "PascalCase"
if stem[0].islower() and any(c.isupper() for c in stem) and "-" not in stem:
    return "camelCase"
if stem.islower() and "-" not in stem:
    return "camelCase"      # <--- THIS IS WRONG
return None
```

The final classified case (`stem.islower() and "-" not in stem`) catches `snake_case` filenames like `my_utils` and labels them `"camelCase"`. A file named `my_utils.py` would be classified as camelCase. This means a directory full of `snake_case` Python files will show zero naming inconsistencies (all classified the same), which is correct by accident for pure-Python projects, but it means a mix of `snake_case` and `camelCase` files gets reported as "all camelCase" with inflated majority counts and wrong labels. The fix would be to either add a proper `snake_case` return value or return `None` for all-lowercase stems (since snake_case vs flat lowercase is ambiguous).

### 1b. Complexity: `compute` function threshold logic inconsistency

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors/complexity.py`, lines 42-44

```python
if result:
    count, label = result
    excess = max(0, count - sig.threshold) if sig.threshold else count
```

When `sig.threshold` is 0 (falsy), `excess = count` -- the entire count is used. But for pattern-based signals (line 49), `count - sig.threshold` is used when `count > sig.threshold`, so `threshold=0` would mean every match contributes. The problem is that `sig.threshold` could be intentionally set to `0`, meaning "every occurrence counts", but the `if sig.threshold` check uses truthiness, so `threshold=0` takes the `else` branch. This is semantically correct by accident (0 threshold -> count), but the dual codepath is confusing and fragile. If someone sets `threshold=0` on a `compute`-based signal expecting "no threshold subtraction", they get the right behavior, but for the wrong reason.

### 1c. Facade: TS multi-line comment false negative

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors/facade.py`, lines 72-76

```python
for line in lines:
    stripped = line.strip()
    if not stripped or stripped.startswith("//") or stripped.startswith("/*"):
        continue
```

This skips lines starting with `/*` but does not track whether we are *inside* a multi-line block comment. A file like:

```typescript
/*
This is a multi-line
comment that spans
several lines
*/
export * from './foo';
```

Would fail detection because lines 2-4 ("This is a multi-line", "comment that spans", "several lines") would hit the `return None` bail-out ("not a re-export line"). In practice, most barrel files do not have multi-line comments, so this is low-severity, but it is a definite false negative.

### 1d. Orphaned: overly broad dynamic import matching

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors/orphaned.py`, lines 18-29

```python
for target in dynamic_targets:
    resolved = alias_resolver(target) if alias_resolver else target
    resolved = resolved.lstrip("./")
    if resolved == name_no_ext or resolved == r:
        return True
    if name_no_ext.endswith("/" + resolved) or name_no_ext.endswith(resolved):
        return True
    if resolved.endswith("/" + stem) or resolved == stem:
        return True
    if resolved.endswith("/" + Path(filepath).name):
        return True
```

The check `name_no_ext.endswith(resolved)` without requiring a `/` prefix means a dynamic target like `"utils"` would match any file ending in `utils` -- e.g. `my_utils`, `test_utils`, `string_utils`. Similarly, `resolved == stem` means a dynamic import of `"index"` would suppress orphan detection for *every* file named `index.py`/`index.ts`. These are potential false negative sources. The `stem` check in particular is very broad.

### 1e. Signature variance: `startswith("test")` is too broad

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors/signature.py`, line 46

```python
if fn.name.startswith("test_") or fn.name.startswith("test"):
    continue  # Skip test functions
```

`fn.name.startswith("test")` will skip any function whose name begins with "test", including real functions like `testConnection`, `testify`, `testimonial_handler`. The `test_` check is sufficient for Python conventions; the bare `test` check is too aggressive for cross-language use (TypeScript functions like `testApiResponse` would be skipped).

---

## 2. Inconsistencies

### 2a. Return signature inconsistency

Most detectors return `tuple[list[dict], int]` (entries, count), but the second element has inconsistent semantics:

| Detector | Second return value |
|---|---|
| `detect_large_files` | total files checked |
| `detect_complexity` | total files checked |
| `detect_duplicates` | total functions |
| `detect_gods` | total classes checked |
| `detect_flat_dirs` | total directories |
| `detect_cycles` | total files in graph |
| `detect_coupling_violations` | cross-boundary edges (not files) |
| `detect_orphaned_files` | total files in graph |
| `detect_signature_variance` | total functions checked |
| `detect_reexport_facades` | total files checked |

The coupling detectors return edge counts rather than file/node counts. This is not wrong per se, but it is inconsistent with the pattern. Every call site discards this value with `_, _ = ...` anyway, so the inconsistency causes no bugs.

### 2b. `path` parameter: sometimes required, sometimes unused

Several detectors take a `path: Path` argument that they never use:

- **`coupling.py`**: All three functions take `path: Path` but never reference it. The graph is the actual data source.
- **`orphaned.py`**: `detect_orphaned_files` takes `path` and uses it only to call `dynamic_import_finder(path, extensions)`. If `dynamic_import_finder` is None, `path` is unused.

Others like `detect_large_files` and `detect_complexity` take `path` and pass it to `file_finder(path)`. The inconsistency is whether the detector or the caller is responsible for file discovery.

### 2c. No base class or protocol for detectors

Despite having a `base.py` with dataclasses, there is no `BaseDetector` class or `Protocol` that detectors implement. Each detector is a standalone function with ad-hoc signatures. The dataclasses in `base.py` are *data transfer objects*, not an abstraction hierarchy. This means:

- No enforced return type (all return `tuple[list[dict], int]` by convention only)
- No standard way to register detectors
- The `__init__.py` is nearly empty (just a docstring) and does not re-export anything

This is more of a design observation than a bug -- the function-based approach is pragmatic and works, but there is no compiler enforcing the contract.

### 2d. `_classify_params` is exported with underscore prefix

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors/passthrough.py`

The function `_classify_params` has a leading underscore (private convention) but is imported and used by both `lang/typescript/extractors.py` and `lang/python/extractors.py`. Either it should be renamed to `classify_params` or accessed differently. This is a naming convention violation.

---

## 3. False Positive / False Negative Risks

### 3a. Duplicate detection: normalized body comparison without context

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors/dupes.py`, line 86

```python
ratio = difflib.SequenceMatcher(None, fa.normalized, fb.normalized).ratio()
```

`SequenceMatcher` with `None` as the junk heuristic means it uses its built-in "popular element" heuristic, which can auto-discard common characters, potentially inflating similarity scores for short-ish functions. For code comparison, `autojunk=False` would be more deterministic:

```python
ratio = difflib.SequenceMatcher(None, fa.normalized, fb.normalized, autojunk=False).ratio()
```

### 3b. Single-use abstraction: barrel file bypass is filename-only

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors/single_use.py`, line 33

```python
if basename in barrel_names:
    continue
```

This only checks the filename, not the directory structure. A file named `index.ts` deep in a subdirectory might legitimately be a single-use abstraction worth flagging, but it will always be skipped because its basename matches a barrel name.

### 3c. Coupling: hardcoded `src/tools/` prefix in output

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors/coupling.py`, line 89

```python
"sole_tool": f"src/tools/{list(tool_areas)[0]}",
```

This hardcodes `"src/tools/"` in the output, which only makes sense for one specific project layout. If `tools_prefix` is e.g. `/abs/path/to/project/lib/tools/`, the output would still say `src/tools/X`. This should use the `tools_prefix` or `rel()` for consistency.

### 3d. Facade: Python docstring detection is incomplete

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors/facade.py`, line 35

```python
elif isinstance(node, ast.Expr) and isinstance(node.value, (ast.Constant, ast.JoinedStr)):
    continue  # docstring
```

This treats any expression statement containing a `Constant` or `JoinedStr` as a docstring. While module-level string expressions are indeed docstrings, this also skips f-string expressions (`JoinedStr`) that could indicate real code (e.g., a standalone `f"Side effect: {register()}"` at module level). This is a very minor false negative risk since standalone f-string expressions at module level are extremely rare.

---

## 4. Performance

### 4a. Duplicate detection: O(n^2) pairwise comparison

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors/dupes.py`, lines 74-89

```python
for i_pos in range(len(large_idx)):
    for j_pos in range(i_pos + 1, len(large_idx)):
```

This is the expected O(n^2) near-duplicate scan. The `fb.loc > fa.loc * 1.5: break` optimization prunes the search space significantly when functions are sorted by size, but for large codebases with many similarly-sized functions (e.g., 500 functions all between 15-22 lines), this will compare every pair. The `SequenceMatcher.ratio()` call itself is O(n*m) in the function body length. For a codebase with 1000 functions of similar size, this is ~500k ratio computations.

The caller in `base.py` does zone-based filtering first, which helps, but this is still the hottest path in the framework.

### 4b. Facade directory detection: redundant file reads

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors/facade.py`, lines 177-181

```python
total_loc = sum(
    len(Path(f).read_text().splitlines())
    for f in files
    if Path(f).exists()
)
```

These files were already read by `_is_py_facade` earlier in the same scan. The content is not cached, so each file is read twice: once for facade classification and once for LOC counting. Also, `Path(f).exists()` is a separate syscall before `read_text()`, which would raise `OSError` anyway if the file does not exist. No `UnicodeDecodeError` handling here, unlike most other file reads.

### 4c. Large file detection: redundant Path construction

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors/large.py`, line 22

```python
p = Path(filepath) if Path(filepath).is_absolute() else PROJECT_ROOT / filepath
```

`Path(filepath)` is constructed twice: once for the `is_absolute()` check and once for the result. This is trivial but appears identically in `complexity.py` line 27. A minor cleanup would be:

```python
p = Path(filepath)
if not p.is_absolute():
    p = PROJECT_ROOT / p
```

---

## 5. Dead Code

### 5a. `reexport_re` in facade.py is largely redundant

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors/facade.py`, lines 69-70

```python
export_re = re.compile(r"""^export\s+(?:\{[^}]*\}|\*)\s+from\s+['"]([^'"]+)['"]""")
reexport_re = re.compile(r"""^export\s+(?:type\s+)?\{[^}]*\}\s+from\s+['"]([^'"]+)['"]""")
```

`export_re` already matches `export { ... } from '...'`. The only thing `reexport_re` adds is `export type { ... } from '...'` support. But since both are tried with `or` on line 77, and `export_re` will match any `export { ... } from`, `reexport_re` only fires when `export_re` does not match. The only case where `export_re` fails but `reexport_re` succeeds is `export type { Foo } from './bar'` -- because `export_re` requires `\{[^}]*\}|\*` without the `type\s+` in between. So `reexport_re` is not dead code, but the naming is confusing -- `export_re` should be `value_export_re` and `reexport_re` should be `type_export_re` or they should be combined into a single regex: `^export\s+(?:type\s+)?(?:\{[^}]*\}|\*)\s+from\s+['"]([^'"]+)['"]`.

### 5b. `__init__.py` is effectively dead

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors/__init__.py`

This file contains only a docstring. It does not export `base.py`'s dataclasses or any detectors. All imports throughout the codebase use explicit paths like `from ..detectors.dupes import detect_duplicates`. The `__init__.py` serves only to make the directory a package, which is its job, but the docstring is aspirational -- it claims the modules export `detect_*` and `cmd_*` functions, but there are no `cmd_*` functions in any detector module (those live in `lang/*/commands.py`).

---

## 6. Abstraction Issues

### 6a. Mixed abstraction levels in the detector suite

The detectors operate at three different abstraction levels:

1. **File-level** (`large.py`, `complexity.py`, `flat_dirs.py`, `naming.py`): Take `path + file_finder`, iterate files, return results. Self-contained.
2. **Graph-level** (`graph.py`, `coupling.py`, `orphaned.py`, `single_use.py`, `facade.py`): Take a pre-built dependency graph. Cannot work without the graph infrastructure.
3. **Entity-level** (`gods.py`, `dupes.py`, `signature.py`): Take pre-extracted class/function lists. Cannot work without the language-specific extractors.

This is actually a reasonable layering, but it means there is no single "run all detectors" entry point in this module. The orchestration lives entirely in `lang/python/__init__.py` and `lang/typescript/__init__.py`, which have nearly-identical phase structures that could benefit from being in the shared `lang/base.py`.

### 6b. `passthrough.py` exports a private function

As noted in 2d, `_classify_params` is imported by both language extractors. The module also exports `classify_passthrough_tier` (public) but the core classification logic is in the private function. The module straddles the line between being a detector (it has `classify_passthrough_tier`) and being a utility (it has `_classify_params`). Neither function follows the `detect_*` -> `(entries, count)` pattern of other detectors.

### 6c. `GodRule.extract` is unconstrained

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors/base.py`, line 63

```python
extract: Callable  # (ClassInfo) -> int
```

The type annotation is just `Callable` with no signature constraint. The comment says `(ClassInfo) -> int` but nothing enforces this. A `Protocol` or `Callable[[ClassInfo], int]` would be more precise.

---

## 7. Naming Issues

### 7a. `gods.py` -- amusing but search-unfriendly

The filename `gods.py` for "god class detection" is clever shorthand but could confuse a newcomer searching the codebase for "god object" or "large class" detection. The function name `detect_gods` similarly reads oddly. `god_classes.py` / `detect_god_classes` would be clearer.

### 7b. `passthrough.py` -- unclear what it detects

Without reading the docstring, `passthrough.py` could mean logging passthrough, network passthrough, or authentication passthrough. `prop_forwarding.py` or `param_passthrough.py` would be more specific.

### 7c. `base.py` is not a base class

`base.py` conventionally implies a base class that others inherit from. This file contains only dataclasses (DTOs). `types.py` or `models.py` would better describe its contents.

### 7d. `rel()` vs `rel(target)` inconsistency in coupling output

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors/coupling.py`

In `detect_coupling_violations`, `entry["target"]` is `rel(target)` (relative path), but `entry["file"]` is the raw `filepath` (absolute path). Same in `detect_cross_tool_imports`. This asymmetry means consumers see mixed absolute/relative paths in the same dict.

---

## Summary of Severity

**Should fix:**
1. Naming detector misclassifies `snake_case` as `camelCase` (1a) -- produces wrong labels in output
2. Signature variance skips non-test functions starting with "test" (1e) -- false negatives
3. `_classify_params` exported with underscore prefix (2d) -- API hygiene
4. Hardcoded `src/tools/` in coupling output (3c) -- wrong for non-standard layouts
5. Facade directory detection lacks `UnicodeDecodeError` handling (4b) -- potential crash

**Should consider:**
1. Orphaned file dynamic import matching is overly broad (1d)
2. SequenceMatcher autojunk in dupe detection (3a)
3. Combine or rename the two TS facade regexes (5a)
4. Mixed absolute/relative paths in coupling output (7d)
5. Redundant file reads in facade directory detection (4b)

**Nice to have:**
1. Typed `Callable` signatures on `GodRule.extract` and `ComplexitySignal.compute` (6c)
2. Rename `base.py` to `types.py` (7c)
3. Fix `__init__.py` docstring claiming `cmd_*` functions exist (5b)
4. Combine the redundant `Path(filepath)` construction in large.py/complexity.py (4c)</result>

# Deep Code Review: CLI and Commands Layer

## Critical Issues

### 1. State loaded twice -- preloaded state ignored by every command
**Files:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/cli.py` (lines 238-243) and every command file
**Severity:** HIGH

In `cli.py` `main()`, state is loaded at line 240 and stored on `args._preloaded_state` at line 242. However, **every single command** (scan, status, show, next, resolve, ignore, plan, fix, zone) calls `load_state(sp)` again internally, re-reading the JSON from disk. The preloaded state is never consumed by any command except `_report_dry_run` in `fix_cmd.py` (line 121).

This means:
- State is deserialized from disk twice on every invocation -- a wasted IO + parse cycle.
- The exclusions applied in `_apply_persisted_exclusions` (line 241) operate on the discarded copy. The commands load their own fresh copy, but by then `set_exclusions()` has already been called from the first copy's config, so the side effect persists. This works by accident, not by design.
- If a command modifies state and saves it (resolve, ignore, scan, fix, zone set/clear), the preloaded copy on `args` is stale.

**Suggested fix:** Either pass the preloaded state into commands (refactor command signatures to accept state), or remove the preload entirely and let commands load their own state. The current hybrid is the worst of both worlds.

---

### 2. `_ResultsWithMeta` class-level mutable default is shared across instances
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/fix_cmd.py` (line 184-185)
**Severity:** HIGH

```python
class _ResultsWithMeta(list):
    skip_reasons: dict[str, int] = {}
```

The `skip_reasons` dict is a **class variable**, meaning it is shared across all instances. If `_wrap_unused_vars_fix` is called multiple times in a session (unlikely but possible in testing or future refactors), the second call's `skip_reasons` will accumulate on top of the first's. This is the classic mutable class-variable pitfall.

Furthermore, in `_apply_and_report` (line 99):
```python
skip_reasons = getattr(results, "skip_reasons", {})
```
This reads the class-level dict (which works), but the `getattr` fallback `{}` would never trigger since the class always has `skip_reasons`.

**Suggested fix:** Set `skip_reasons` in `__init__`:
```python
class _ResultsWithMeta(list):
    def __init__(self, *args, **kwargs):
        super().__init__(*args, **kwargs)
        self.skip_reasons: dict[str, int] = {}
```

---

### 3. `cmd_scan` loads state twice, discarding the one with `--exclude` config
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/scan.py` (lines 164-165)
**Severity:** HIGH

`cmd_scan` calls `load_state(sp)` at line 165, getting a fresh copy. But in `cli.py:main()`, state was already loaded at line 240, `--exclude` was persisted to `state.config.exclude` (not saved yet), and exclusions were applied. The `cmd_scan` loads a fresh copy from disk that does **not** have the CLI `--exclude` merged into `config.exclude`. Then at line 171-172, it sets `state["config"]["exclude"]` again on this fresh copy. This works, but the double-load is confusing and fragile.

---

## High Severity Issues

### 4. `_show_dry_run_samples` miscalculates skipped count
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/fix_cmd.py` (lines 227-237)
**Severity:** HIGH

```python
def _show_dry_run_samples(entries, results):
    ...
    skipped = sum(len(r["removed"]) for r in results)
    if len(entries) > skipped:
        print(c(f"\n  Note: {len(entries) - skipped} of {len(entries)} entries were skipped ..."
```

Variable `skipped` is named "skipped" but it actually computes the **total items fixed** (sum of all removed items across results). Then it checks if `entries > skipped`, i.e., "if there are more entries than items fixed." The message says "X entries were skipped" which is correct in meaning, but the variable name `skipped` is deeply misleading -- it holds the count of items that were *not* skipped.

**Suggested fix:** Rename to `total_fixed = sum(...)` and `skipped = len(entries) - total_fixed`.

---

### 5. `cmd_detect` hardcodes TypeScript as default language
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/detect.py` (lines 16-19)
**Severity:** HIGH (UX)

```python
if not lang:
    from ..lang import get_lang
    lang = get_lang("typescript")
```

When `_resolve_lang` returns `None` (auto-detection found no language), detect silently defaults to TypeScript. This will produce confusing errors when a user in a Python-only project runs `desloppify detect logs` without `--lang`. The auto-detection in `_resolve_lang` already handles this case and exits with a helpful message -- but `cmd_detect` overrides that fallback.

**Suggested fix:** Either remove the TypeScript default and let the user get the auto-detection error, or at minimum print a warning that TypeScript was assumed.

---

### 6. `_get_fixer` always imports TypeScript fixers -- hardcoded language
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/fix_cmd.py` (lines 152-182)
**Severity:** HIGH

```python
def _get_fixer(name: str) -> dict | None:
    from ..lang.typescript import fixers as F
```

The built-in fixer registry unconditionally imports TypeScript fixers and detectors (`_detect_unused`, `_detect_logs`, `_detect_dead_exports`, `_detect_smell_flat` all import from `..lang.typescript`). If a user runs `desloppify fix unused-imports` on a Python project without `--lang`, the language plugin path at line 44-48 of `_load_fixer` might find no match in `lang.fixers`, fall through to `_get_fixer`, and then import TypeScript modules. This would either crash (missing TS files) or produce nonsensical results.

**Suggested fix:** The fallback registry should be guarded by a language check, or moved into the TypeScript language plugin's `fixers` dict.

---

### 7. `move.py` string `.replace()` is unsafe for import path updates
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/move.py` (lines 147-157)
**Severity:** HIGH

```python
for old_str, new_str in self_changes:
    content = content.replace(old_str, new_str)
```

Using `str.replace()` does a global substring replacement, not a targeted one. If an import path like `./foo` appears in a comment, string literal, or multiple import lines with different contexts, all occurrences will be replaced. This is also done for importer changes at line 155.

The same pattern repeats in `_cmd_move_dir` at lines 384 and 392.

**Suggested fix:** Use line-by-line replacement restricted to import-statement lines, or use a regex that matches only within import/require/from statements.

---

### 8. No validation of `--count` parameter bounds in `next` command
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/next.py` (line 24)
**Severity:** MEDIUM

```python
count = getattr(args, "count", 1) or 1
```

No upper bound. A user could pass `--count 100000` and get a massive terminal dump. Not dangerous, but poor UX.

**Suggested fix:** Clamp to a reasonable max (e.g., 50) or add a note in help text.

---

## Medium Severity Issues

### 9. `resolve` command: `args.status` naming conflicts with argparse "status" concept
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/resolve.py` (line 151 of cli.py, line 13 of resolve.py)
**Severity:** MEDIUM (UX confusion)

The `resolve` subparser's first positional argument is named `status` (the target status like "fixed"/"wontfix"). But the `show` command also has a `--status` flag (the filter status). This is confusing:
- `desloppify resolve fixed "logs::*"` -- "fixed" is a target status
- `desloppify show --status fixed` -- "fixed" is a filter

The help text is clear enough, but the shared name creates cognitive overhead when reading code.

---

### 10. `cmd_scan` calls `lang.file_finder(path)` up to 3 times
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/scan.py` (lines 191, 206-207)
**Severity:** MEDIUM (performance)

```python
# Line 191: inside _audit_excluded_dirs
scanned_files = lang.file_finder(path)

# Line 206-207: full scan detection
all_files = lang.file_finder(PROJECT_ROOT)
scanned_files = lang.file_finder(path)  # called again!
```

`file_finder(path)` is called twice with the same path (once for the stale-exclude audit, once for the full-scan check). Depending on whether `file_finder` has caching, this could be a significant cost for large projects.

**Suggested fix:** Cache the result of `lang.file_finder(path)` once at the top of `cmd_scan`.

---

### 11. `_show_post_scan_analysis` return type annotation doesn't match actual return
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/scan.py` (line 121)
**Severity:** MEDIUM

Signature says: `-> tuple[list[str], str | None, dict]` (3-element tuple)

But the function returns: `return warnings, narrative` (2-element tuple)

The unpacking at line 250 also expects 2 values: `warnings, narrative = _show_post_scan_analysis(...)`. So it works at runtime, but the type annotation is wrong and misleading.

**Suggested fix:** Change annotation to `-> tuple[list[str], dict]`.

---

### 12. `_cascade_import_cleanup` runs fixers even in dry-run context
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/fix_cmd.py` (line 263)
**Severity:** MEDIUM

The function signature accepts `dry_run: bool` and passes it through to `fix_unused_imports`, but it is only ever called from `_apply_and_report` (line 96) with `dry_run=False`:

```python
fixer["post_fix"](path, state, prev_score, False)
```

The `dry_run` parameter in the signature is unused dead functionality. More importantly, the `post_fix` hook is only called inside `_apply_and_report`, which is the non-dry-run path. So the cascade never runs during dry-run. This is correct behavior but the dead `dry_run` parameter is misleading.

---

### 13. `show` command: `--chronic` flag can combine confusingly with a pattern
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/show.py` (lines 73-86)
**Severity:** MEDIUM (UX)

```python
if chronic:
    matches = [f for f in state["findings"].values()
               if f.get("reopen_count", 0) >= 2 and f["status"] == "open"]
    status_filter = "open"
    pattern = pattern or "<chronic>"
```

If the user passes both `--chronic` and a pattern, the pattern is ignored silently. `desloppify show logs --chronic` would show ALL chronic findings, not just chronic "logs" findings. The pattern argument is only used for display: `pattern = pattern or "<chronic>"`.

**Suggested fix:** Either reject the combination with an error, or apply the pattern as an additional filter on chronic findings.

---

### 14. Inconsistent "no scans yet" check across commands
**Files:** Multiple
**Severity:** MEDIUM (UX inconsistency)

- `show.py` line 64: checks `if not state.get("last_scan")` and returns early
- `plan_cmd.py` line 17: checks `if not state.get("last_scan")` and returns early
- `status.py` line 31: checks `if not state.get("last_scan")` and returns early (but only when not `--json`)
- `next.py`: does NOT check for no scans. Will just say "Nothing to do!" which is misleading.
- `resolve.py`: does NOT check. Will say "No open findings matching: ..." which is technically true but unhelpful.
- `scan.py`: does not need the check (it creates the first scan).

**Suggested fix:** Add the "no scans yet" check to `next` and `resolve` for consistency, or centralize it.

---

### 15. `_move_ts.py` uses `SRC_PATH` global which is hardcoded from env
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/_move_ts.py` (line 6)
**Severity:** MEDIUM

```python
from ..utils import SRC_PATH
```

And from `utils.py`:
```python
SRC_PATH = PROJECT_ROOT / os.environ.get("DESLOPPIFY_SRC", "src")
```

The TS move command uses `SRC_PATH` to compute `@/` alias paths. This is a module-level constant set at import time from an environment variable. If the project's source root isn't `src/` and `DESLOPPIFY_SRC` isn't set, all `@/` alias computations will be wrong. The language config already has `default_src` which could be used instead.

**Suggested fix:** Pass the actual source path from the language config rather than relying on the module-level constant.

---

### 16. `resolve` command: `args.status` positional ordering is error-prone
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/cli.py` (lines 150-155)
**Severity:** MEDIUM (UX)

```python
p_resolve.add_argument("status", choices=["fixed", "wontfix", "false_positive"])
p_resolve.add_argument("patterns", nargs="+", ...)
```

The status comes before patterns: `desloppify resolve fixed "logs::src/foo.tsx::*"`. But the usage examples at line 83 show: `desloppify resolve fixed "unused::src/foo.tsx::React"`. This is internally consistent, but easy to confuse with other CLI tools where the verb typically comes last. A user might type `desloppify resolve "logs::*" fixed` and get a confusing argparse error about invalid choices.

---

### 17. `plan_cmd` does not write `_write_query`
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/plan_cmd.py`
**Severity:** MEDIUM (inconsistency)

Every other query-like command (scan, status, show, next, resolve, ignore, fix) writes to `query.json` via `_write_query`. The `plan` command does not. The import is `from ..cli import _state_path` (no `_write_query`). This means an LLM reading `query.json` after a `plan` command will see stale data from the previous command.

**Suggested fix:** Add a `_write_query({"command": "plan", ...})` call.

---

### 18. `move` command does not update state after moving files
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/move.py`
**Severity:** MEDIUM

After moving a file and updating imports, the desloppify state file is not updated. All finding IDs contain file paths (e.g., `unused::src/foo.tsx::React`). After a move, these IDs become stale, pointing to non-existent paths. The user must run `desloppify scan` to fix this, but there's no warning about it.

**Suggested fix:** Print a warning like "Run `desloppify scan` to update state with new file paths" after every move.

---

## Low Severity Issues

### 19. Dead import: `json` in `next.py`
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/next.py` (line 3)
**Severity:** LOW

```python

```

`json` is imported at module level but only used inside the `if output_file:` branch (line 53), where it is used as `json.dumps`. Not "dead" per se, but the import is at the top of the file while `Path` is also imported. No issue at runtime, just inconsistency with other commands that lazy-import.

### 20. `_audit_excluded_dirs` does full file reads to check for references
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/scan.py` (lines 30-37)
**Severity:** LOW (performance)

For each excluded directory, the function reads every scanned file's full content to check if the directory name appears as a substring. This is O(N * file_size) per excluded directory. For large codebases, this could be slow. Also, the check `if ex_dir in content` is a substring match -- a directory named "api" would match any occurrence of "api" in the file, including strings like "capitalize" or "api_key", producing false negatives for the "stale" detection.

**Suggested fix:** Use a more targeted search (import-statement parsing, or at least word-boundary matching).

---

### 21. `_format_delta` produces confusing output for zero delta
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/scan.py` (line 89)
**Severity:** LOW

```python

```

When `prev` is `None` (first scan), `delta` is forced to 0, so no delta string is shown. But the actual new score could be e.g., 72.5 -- the user sees "Health: 72.5/100" with no indication that this is the first measurement. This is intentional (no prior to compare to), but slightly confusing on first use.

### 22. `_cmd_move_dir` uses `source_path` variable from outer scope
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/move.py` (line 381)
**Severity:** LOW (fragile)

```python

```

`source_path` is defined at line 223 as `Path(source_abs)`. By the time we reach line 381, the directory has already been moved (line 371: `shutil.move(source_abs, dest_abs)`). So `source_path` no longer exists on disk. This works because `relative_to` is a string operation that doesn't check the filesystem, but it's fragile -- a future refactor that calls `resolve()` on `source_path` would break.

### 23. `_apply_and_report` score display uses integer formatting for floats
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/fix_cmd.py` (lines 89-93)


No `.1f` formatting on delta or score. Compare with `resolve.py` line 37-38 which also uses raw formatting, but `scan.py` `_format_delta` uses `:.1f`. The score display formatting is inconsistent across commands:
- `scan.py`: `{new_obj:.1f}/100` (1 decimal)
- `fix_cmd.py`: `{state['score']}/100` (no formatting)
- `resolve.py`: `{state['score']}/100` (no formatting)
- `status.py`: `{score}/100` (no formatting) vs `{obj_score:.1f}/100` (1 decimal for objective)

**Suggested fix:** Standardize score formatting to always use `.1f`.

### 24. `_show_dry_run_samples` seed is hardcoded
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/fix_cmd.py` (line 229)
**Severity:** LOW

The random seed is hardcoded so dry-run samples are deterministic. This is actually good for reproducibility, but it means repeated dry-runs always show the same sample files, potentially hiding issues in other files.

### 25. `zone` command has no `required=True` on subparsers
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/cli.py` (line 198)

Unlike the top-level subparsers which have `required=True` (line 106), the zone subparsers don't. Running `desloppify zone` without a subcommand prints "Usage: desloppify zone {show|set|clear}" (line 20 of `zone_cmd.py`), which is fine, but it would be cleaner to let argparse handle this with `required=True` and get a standard error message.

### 26. `detect` command does not write `_write_query`

Similar to issue #17 with `plan`, the `detect` command does not write to `query.json`. It delegates entirely to `lang.detect_commands[detector](args)`, so the LLM won't find structured output in the query file after a detect run.

### 27. `_resolve_lang` called repeatedly across command flow
**File:** Multiple
**Severity:** LOW (performance, minor)

`_resolve_lang(args)` is called from `cli.py:main()` at line 231, and then again in each command that needs the language. The function does auto-detection, which walks the filesystem. It's called: once in `main()`, once in `cmd_scan`, once inside `_show_post_scan_analysis` via `compute_narrative`, once in `_show_score_delta`, etc. Each call re-runs auto-detection from scratch.

**Suggested fix:** Cache the result on `args._lang` after the first call.

### 28. `_warn_uncommitted_changes` suggests `git add -A` which could stage secrets
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/fix_cmd.py` (lines 216-225)
**Severity:** LOW (UX)


Suggesting `git add -A` blindly could stage sensitive files (.env, credentials). Better to suggest `git add .` or `git stash`.

### 29. `next` command resolve hint shows wrong argument order
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/next.py` (lines 92-94)
**Severity:** LOW (UX)


But the actual argparse definition has `status` before `patterns`:
```python

p_resolve.add_argument("patterns", nargs="+", ...)
```

So the correct syntax is `desloppify resolve fixed "ID"`, not `desloppify resolve "ID" fixed`. The hint will cause a parse error.

**Suggested fix:** Change to:
```python

```

### 30. Usage examples in `cli.py` also show wrong argument order for `resolve`
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/cli.py` (line 83)
**Severity:** LOW (but matches #29)

```

```

This one is actually **correct** -- status first, then patterns. But the `next.py` hint in issue #29 reverses them. Inconsistency between the help text (correct) and the runtime hint (wrong).

### 31. `_move_py.py` does not handle `import X.Y.Z` form (only `from ... import`)
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/_move_py.py` (line 159)
**Severity:** LOW

The relative import regex at line 159 only handles `from .X import ...`:
```python

```

Bare `import .foo` is not valid Python syntax, so this is correct for relative imports. But for absolute imports (line 152), the `_has_exact_module` check looks at the whole line, which should handle `import foo.bar` and `from foo.bar import baz`. This is fine.

### 32. `move.py` does not validate that source and dest are different
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/move.py`
**Severity:** LOW

If a user runs `desloppify move foo.py foo.py`, the code will resolve both to the same absolute path but there's no explicit check. `shutil.move` to the same path might work or might corrupt the file depending on the OS. The `dest_abs == source_abs` case should be caught early.

---

## Summary of Cross-Cutting Concerns

**State loading pattern:** Every command independently calls `load_state()` + `_state_path()`, creating a redundant pattern. The preloaded state in `cli.py` is largely unused, adding confusion.

**Score formatting:** Inconsistent between commands. Scan uses `:.1f`, fix and resolve use raw formatting, status uses both depending on which score type is available.

**Narrative/query pattern:** Most commands follow a consistent pattern of computing narrative and writing query.json, but `plan` and `detect` skip this. The pattern itself is boilerplate-heavy (each command imports `compute_narrative`, `_resolve_lang`, calls them, and passes to `_write_query`).

**Language resolution:** Called repeatedly without caching. The "default to TypeScript" behavior in `detect.py` is inconsistent with other commands that exit with an error when no language is detected.

**Error handling on sys.exit:** Some commands call `sys.exit(1)` on errors (detect, fix, resolve-wontfix-no-note), others just `return` (show, plan, status). The distinction seems arbitrary -- `sys.exit` is used for validation errors, `return` for "nothing to show" cases, which is reasonable but not documented.</result>

# Deep Code Review: Python Language Plugin

## 1. Bugs and Logic Errors

### 1.1 `extract_py_functions` never populates `params`, breaking signature variance detection

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/extractors.py`, line 83

`FunctionInfo` has a `params` field, but `extract_py_functions()` never sets it:

```python
functions.append(FunctionInfo(
    name=name, file=filepath, line=start_line + 1,
    end_line=end_line, loc=end_line - start_line, body=body,
    normalized=normalized,
    body_hash=hashlib.md5(normalized.encode()).hexdigest(),
))
```

Meanwhile, `detect_signature_variance()` in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors/signature.py` relies on `fn.params`:

```python
params = [p for p in fn.params if p not in ("self", "cls")]
```

Since `params` defaults to an empty list, every Python function gets `params=[]`, every function has the same empty signature, and the variance check (`len(param_signatures) < 2`) is always true, meaning **signature variance is silently dead for Python**. The detector will never produce results.

**Severity: HIGH** -- an entire detector is non-functional.

### 1.2 `_PY_FUNC_PATTERN` uses `re.DOTALL` making `[^)]` match newlines

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/extractors.py`, lines 249-252

```python
_PY_FUNC_PATTERN = re.compile(
    r"^def\s+(\w+)\s*\(([^)]*)\)\s*(?:->.*?)?:",
    re.MULTILINE | re.DOTALL,
)
```

With `re.DOTALL`, the `[^)]*` in group 2 matches newlines, so for multi-line signatures, it captures everything up to the closing `)`. That much is fine. However, the `.*?` in `(?:->.*?)?:` is also affected by `DOTALL`. For a function like:

```python
def foo(x) -> dict:
    d = {}
    d["a"] = 1
    return d

def bar():
```

The `->.*?:` will lazily match `-> dict:` which works. But consider:

```python
def foo(x) -> dict[
    str, int
]:
```

Here `[^)]*` won't match past the first `)` in the return annotation. The regex simply fails to match functions whose return annotations contain `)`. This is a known limitation of regex-based parsing, but it means passthrough detection misses any function with `-> tuple[str, int]`, `-> Optional[dict[str, Any]]`, etc.

**Severity: MEDIUM** -- passthrough detection misses functions with complex return annotations.

### 1.3 `compute_max_params` regex misses multi-line signatures

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/detectors/complexity.py`, line 11

```python
param_re = re.compile(r"def\s+\w+\s*\(([^)]*)\)", re.DOTALL)
```

The `re.DOTALL` makes `[^)]*` match newlines, so multi-line signatures DO work here. However, the param parsing logic is naive:

```python
params = [p.strip() for p in m.group(1).split(",") if p.strip()]
```

This breaks on parameters with default values containing commas: `def f(x: dict[str, int] = {})`. The split on `,` produces fragments like `x: dict[str` and `int] = {}`. The `real_params` filter (`not p.startswith("*")`) doesn't catch these fragments, so the param count is inflated.

**Severity: LOW** -- inflates param count for functions with generic type annotations, making the complexity signal fire more aggressively. Since this is a heuristic threshold (>7), occasional over-counting is tolerable.

### 1.4 `_find_signature_end` loose match for signature closing

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/extractors.py`, lines 36-44

```python
def _find_signature_end(lines: list[str], start: int) -> int | None:
    for j in range(start, len(lines)):
        lt = lines[j]
        if ")" in lt and ":" in lt[lt.rindex(")") + 1:]:
            return j
        if j > start and lt.strip().endswith(":"):
            return j
    return None
```

The second branch (`lt.strip().endswith(":")`) catches lines that end with `:` but have no `)`. This could false-match a decorator or annotation line that ends with `:`, particularly inside a multi-line signature. For example:

```python
def my_func(
    x: int,     # this line ends with ,
    y: dict[str:int],  # using slice notation in type hint would match
):
```

Admittedly `dict[str:int]` is invalid Python, but `y: int  # some note:` would match the endswith(":") check. This is mitigated by `j > start` so it only matches continuation lines.

**Severity: LOW** -- unlikely to cause real issues in practice.

### 1.5 Class regex doesn't handle multi-line base class lists

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/extractors.py`, line 146

```python
class_re = re.compile(r"^class\s+(\w+)\s*(?:\(([^)]*)\))?\s*:")
```

This regex requires the entire base class list to be on one line. A class like:

```python
class MyView(
    LoginRequiredMixin,
    PermissionRequiredMixin,
    DetailView,
):
```

will not be captured because `[^)]*` without `DOTALL` won't cross lines, and the regex is applied per-line with `re.match`. The class will be silently skipped by the god class detector.

**Severity: MEDIUM** -- common Django/Flask pattern. God classes with multi-line inheritance (often the most complex ones) will not be detected.

### 1.6 `_extract_init_attributes` only enters `in_init` from `stripped` but checks indent from `lines[k]`

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/extractors.py`, lines 207-227

The method checks `re.match(r"def\s+__init__\s*\(", stripped)` but then uses `len(lines[k]) - len(lines[k].lstrip())` for indent tracking. This is correct, but if `__init__` has a multi-line signature:

```python
def __init__(
    self,
    x: int,
    y: int,
):
    self.x = x
```

The code sets `init_indent` to the indent of the `def __init__` line, then on the next lines (`self,`, `x: int,`, etc.) checks if they're at or below that indent. The continuation lines of the signature are indented deeper, so they pass. But it starts looking for `self.x =` immediately, and `self,` inside the signature would be matched by `re.finditer(r"self\.(\w+)\s*=", lines[k])`. Wait -- `self,` doesn't match `self\.\w+\s*=`, so this is actually fine.

**Severity: NONE** -- false alarm, the regex is specific enough.

---

## 2. False Positive / False Negative Risks

### 2.1 `mutable_default` regex misses `dict()` and `list()` calls, catches literals in strings

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/detectors/smells.py`, line 22

```python
_smell("mutable_default", ..., r"def\s+\w+\([^)]*=\s*(?:\[\]|\{\}|set\(\))"),
```

**False negatives:**
- `def f(x=list())` and `def f(x=dict())` are missed. These are common anti-patterns.
- Multi-line signatures are missed (the `[^)]*` won't cross lines without `re.DOTALL`).
- `def f(x=collections.defaultdict(list))` is missed, but this is arguably intentional.

**False positives:**
- The `_match_is_in_string` and `_build_string_line_set` guards mitigate most string false positives.

**Severity: LOW** -- the most common forms (`[]`, `{}`, `set()`) are caught.

### 2.2 `magic_number` regex fires on year literals, port numbers, HTTP status codes

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/detectors/smells.py`, line 28

```python
_smell("magic_number", ..., r"(?:==|!=|>=?|<=?|[+\-*/])\s*\d{4,}"),
```

This fires on `if year >= 2000`, `if status == 8080`, `port + 1000`. Year comparisons and port numbers are extremely common in production code. The threshold of 4+ digits catches 1000 and above, which includes many legitimate constants.

**Severity: LOW** -- it's a `low` severity smell, so the impact is tolerable, but it creates noise.

### 2.3 `dead_function` misses functions with `raise NotImplementedError`

The dead function detector (`_detect_dead_functions`) only flags functions whose body is `pass`, `return`, or `return None` (optionally with a docstring). Abstract methods commonly look like:

```python
def process(self, data):
    raise NotImplementedError
```

These stub-only functions are arguably "dead" too, but they are intentionally not flagged. This seems like a deliberate choice, but worth noting. The decorator check (`if node.decorator_list: return`) correctly exempts `@abstractmethod` decorated functions, but a function with `raise NotImplementedError` and no decorator is a code smell that is missed.

**Severity: LOW** -- more of a feature gap than a bug.

### 2.4 `deferred_import` detector produces false positives for lazy imports by design

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/detectors/smells.py`, lines 325-341

The detector flags any function-level import except those from `typing`, `typing_extensions`, and `__future__`. However, lazy imports are a well-established pattern used deliberately for:
- Performance (avoid importing expensive modules at startup)
- Optional dependencies (`try: import pandas; except ImportError: ...`)
- Plugin systems

This detector will flag every lazy import in the scanned codebase. The irony is that **this codebase itself uses lazy imports extensively** -- see every phase function in `__init__.py` which does `from .detectors.xxx import ...` inside the function body. When scanning itself, it will produce dozens of deferred_import findings.

**Severity: MEDIUM** -- significant noise for codebases that use lazy imports intentionally. Consider adding a heuristic (e.g., only flag when the import is inside a `try` block, or only flag when there are many deferred imports in a single function).

### 2.5 `eval_exec` regex false negative: `builtins.eval()`

The lookbehind `(?<!\.)(?<!\w)` correctly prevents matching `os.system_eval()` or `self.evaluate()`, but it also prevents matching `builtins.eval()` because of the `(?<!\.)` lookbehind. This is actually correct behavior since the dot prefix is excluded -- but `builtins.eval()` is still dangerous. Edge case, but worth noting.

### 2.6 `hardcoded_url` allows URLs in f-strings through

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/detectors/smells.py`, line 30

The pattern `(?:['"])https?://[^\s'"]+(?:['"])` requires the URL to start and end with matching quote characters. But f-string expressions like:

```python
url = f"https://api.example.com/{endpoint}"
```

won't match because the `[^\s'"]+` stops at `{`, and there's no closing quote immediately after. So template URLs in f-strings produce false negatives. Meanwhile, URLs in regular strings are caught. This is probably fine -- template URLs are less "hardcoded" than literal ones.

---

## 3. Edge Cases with Modern Python Syntax

### 3.1 Walrus operator `:=` in function signatures confuses signature end detection

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/extractors.py`

The walrus operator doesn't appear in function signatures, so `_find_signature_end` is unaffected. However, if used in default values (not valid Python, so this is moot).

### 3.2 `match` statement (Python 3.10+) nesting depth

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/detectors/complexity.py`

The nesting depth detector uses indentation, which correctly handles `match`/`case` blocks since they follow normal indentation rules. No issue here.

### 3.3 Decorators with complex expressions

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/extractors.py`, line 55

```python
fn_re = re.compile(r"^(\s*)def\s+(\w+)\s*\(")
```

This correctly anchors to the `def` keyword, so decorators above the function don't interfere. However, the regex-based function extractor doesn't handle `async def`:

```python
async def process(data):
```

The regex `^(\s*)def\s+` won't match `async def`. All async functions are silently skipped by `extract_py_functions`, meaning:
- Async functions are not checked for duplicates
- Async functions are not checked for passthrough forwarding
- Async functions are not extracted for signature variance (which is already broken per issue 1.1)

**Severity: HIGH** -- in async-heavy codebases (FastAPI, aiohttp, etc.), a significant fraction of functions are invisible to the extractor.

### 3.4 Type alias statements (Python 3.12+)

```python
type Point = tuple[int, int]
```

The `type` statement is not handled by any detector, and shouldn't cause false positives since no detector regex matches `type\s+\w+`. No issue.

### 3.5 `except*` (Python 3.11+) exception groups

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/detectors/smells.py`, line 215

```python
if not re.match(r"except\s*(?:\w|:)", stripped) and stripped != "except:":
```

The `except*` syntax (`except* ValueError as eg:`) is not matched by this regex because `*` is not `\w`. So `_walk_except_blocks` skips `except*` blocks entirely. This means empty `except*` blocks and swallowed errors in exception groups are not detected.

**Severity: LOW** -- `except*` is still uncommon, but it will become more common over time.

---

## 4. Inconsistencies

### 4.1 Duplicate import resolution logic in `deps.py` and `smells.py`

`_resolve_import_target()` in `smells.py` (lines 401-429) reimplements the same import resolution that `_resolve_python_import()` in `deps.py` already provides. They have slightly different semantics:
- `deps.py` uses `PROJECT_ROOT` as fallback
- `smells.py` uses `scan_root.parent` as the base for absolute imports

This could lead to inconsistencies where the same import resolves differently in the smells detector vs. the dep graph builder.

### 4.2 `_read_file` helper duplicated vs. inline reads

`extractors.py` has a `_read_file()` helper (line 12), but `smells.py` and `dict_keys.py` each inline their own file-reading patterns:

```python
# smells.py line 165-168
p = Path(filepath) if Path(filepath).is_absolute() else PROJECT_ROOT / filepath
content = p.read_text()

# dict_keys.py line 486-487
p = Path(filepath) if Path(filepath).is_absolute() else PROJECT_ROOT / filepath
source = p.read_text()
```

This is 3 different implementations of the same "resolve and read a file" operation. If `_read_file` were shared (e.g., in utils), the pattern would be consistent and the error handling would be uniform.

### 4.3 `detect_smells` parses AST once in `_detect_ast_smells` and again in `_detect_star_import_no_all`

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/detectors/smells.py`, lines 262-265 and 362-364

Each file is parsed with `ast.parse()` twice per file in `detect_smells()`:
1. Inside `_detect_ast_smells()` (line 264)
2. Inside `_detect_star_import_no_all()` (line 363)

The two functions are called sequentially in the same loop iteration (lines 194-195). This is wasted work.

### 4.4 `_detect_inline_classes` only checks direct children

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/detectors/smells.py`, lines 344-351

```python
def _detect_inline_classes(filepath: str, node: ast.AST, smell_counts: dict[str, list]):
    for child in node.body:
        if isinstance(child, ast.ClassDef):
```

This only checks the immediate body of the function, not nested blocks. A class defined inside an `if` block or `for` loop inside a function would be missed:

```python
def factory():
    if some_condition:
        class InnerClass:
            pass
```

The `_detect_ast_smells` function uses `ast.walk(tree)` to find all functions, but `_detect_inline_classes` then only checks `node.body` (direct children), missing classes nested deeper.

## 5. Performance

### 5.1 Schema drift clustering is O(n^2) with large constant factor

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/detectors/dict_keys.py`, lines 552-567

```python
for i, lit in enumerate(all_literals):
    if assigned[i]:
        continue
    cluster = [lit]
    assigned[i] = True
    for j in range(i + 1, len(all_literals)):
        if assigned[j]:
            continue
        for member in cluster:
            if _jaccard(lit["keys"], all_literals[j]["keys"]) >= 0.8:
```

For each unassigned literal, it checks against all subsequent unassigned literals, and for each, it checks against all existing cluster members. In the worst case (no matches), this is O(n^2). With matches, the inner `for member in cluster` loop makes it worse -- potentially O(n^2 * k) where k is cluster size.

For a large codebase with thousands of dict literals, this could be slow. However, the `>= 0.8` Jaccard threshold means most comparisons exit quickly, and the `break` on match limits cluster growth checking.

Additionally, there is a subtle bug in the clustering: the comparison always uses `lit["keys"]` (the cluster seed) rather than `all_literals[j]["keys"]` against each member. The comment says "Check similarity against any member in the cluster" but the code only checks against `lit` (the seed). This means single-linkage semantics are not actually implemented -- it's seed-linkage. Items similar to non-seed cluster members but not to the seed won't be added.

**Severity: MEDIUM** -- the clustering semantics are wrong (seed-only comparison instead of single-linkage), and performance could degrade on large codebases.

### 5.2 `detect_schema_drift` re-parses all Python files

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/detectors/dict_keys.py`, lines 512-544

`detect_schema_drift()` walks all Python files and `ast.parse()`s them again, even though `detect_dict_key_flow()` already does this and already collects `_dict_literals`. The `_phase_dict_keys` function in `__init__.py` calls both, meaning every file is parsed twice (once per pass).

Looking at the code: `detect_dict_key_flow` already collects `all_literals` (line 482-499) but throws them away -- it only returns `all_findings`. The `DictKeyVisitor` also collects `_dict_literals` but they're never used after the visit. This is wasted work: `detect_schema_drift` re-does the literal collection from scratch.

**Severity: MEDIUM** -- could be fixed by having `detect_dict_key_flow` return the collected literals and passing them to `detect_schema_drift`.

### 5.3 Per-line string set iteration in smells detector

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/detectors/smells.py`, lines 174-182

```python
for check in SMELL_CHECKS:
    if check["pattern"] is None:
        continue
    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
        if i in multiline_string_lines:
            continue
        m = re.search(check["pattern"], line)
```

This iterates all lines for each regex-based smell check. With ~10 regex checks and N lines, this is O(10N) regex executions per file. Pre-compiling the regexes into `re.compile` objects would provide a modest speedup, as `re.search` with a string pattern re-compiles on every call (though Python does cache compiled patterns, the cache has a fixed size of 512).

**Severity: LOW** -- the caching in Python's `re` module mitigates this, but pre-compiling would still be cleaner.

---

## 6. Error Handling

### 6.1 `_levenshtein` is recursive and can stack overflow

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/detectors/dict_keys.py`, lines 39-52

```python
def _levenshtein(s1: str, s2: str) -> int:
    if len(s1) < len(s2):
        return _levenshtein(s2, s1)
```

This only recurses once (to swap arguments), so it won't actually stack overflow. The rest of the function is iterative (dynamic programming). This is fine.

### 6.2 ruff/pyflakes timeout of 60 seconds may be too short for large monorepos

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/detectors/unused.py`, lines 43-49

If ruff or pyflakes times out, the function returns `None`, and `detect_unused` falls through to pyflakes (which also has a 60s timeout). If both time out, it silently returns empty results. There's no warning or log message indicating the timeout.

**Severity: LOW** -- silent failure, but unlikely in practice for typical codebases.

### 6.3 `detect_passthrough_functions` body extraction assumes top-level functions only

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/extractors.py`, lines 270-272

```python
rest = content[m.end():]
bm = re.search(r"\n(?=[^\s\n#])", rest)
body = rest[:bm.start()] if bm else rest
```

The body extraction looks for the next line starting with a non-whitespace character. For top-level functions, this works. But for methods inside classes, the next method's `def` is indented and starts with whitespace, so the "body" extends to the next class-level definition or the end of the class. This means method bodies are over-estimated, potentially including the next method's body. This inflates param occurrence counts and could cause false negatives (parameters appear to be "directly used" when they actually appear in a different method).

The `_PY_FUNC_PATTERN` regex only matches `^def\s+` (anchored to line start with `re.MULTILINE`), so it only captures top-level functions, not methods. Methods are silently skipped. This is actually fine for passthrough detection (methods are less likely to be passthroughs), but it means:
- Methods with many forwarded params are not detected
- The body extraction logic is consistent with the matching scope

**Severity: LOW** -- consistent but limited.

---

## 7. Dead Code and Unused Imports

### 7.1 `re` import in `unused.py` is used

Line 4 of `unused.py` imports `re`, which is used in `_try_ruff` and `_try_pyflakes`. No dead import.

### 7.2 `_READ_METHODS` and `_WRITE_METHODS` in `dict_keys.py` are unused

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/detectors/dict_keys.py`, lines 34-35

```python
_READ_METHODS = {"get", "pop", "setdefault", "__getitem__", "__contains__"}
_WRITE_METHODS = {"update", "setdefault", "__setitem__"}
```

These sets are defined but never referenced anywhere in the code. The method dispatch in `visit_Call` (line 266) uses inline string comparisons instead:

```python
if method in ("get", "pop", "__getitem__", "__contains__"):
```

The sets appear to have been intended as a refactoring aid but were never wired up.

**Severity: LOW** -- dead code, no functional impact. But a maintenance hazard if someone updates the sets thinking they affect behavior.

### 7.3 `_dict_literals` collected but unused in `DictKeyVisitor`

The `DictKeyVisitor` collects `_dict_literals` in both `_check_dict_creation` (line 195) and `visit_Dict` (line 373). These are accumulated per-file in `detect_dict_key_flow` (line 499: `all_literals.extend(visitor._dict_literals)`) but then `all_literals` is never returned or used -- only `all_findings` is returned (line 501). The variable is dead.

This connects to performance issue 5.2: the collection happens but is thrown away, and `detect_schema_drift` re-collects the same data.

### 7.4 `visit_Dict` creates duplicate entries with `_check_dict_creation`

When a dict literal is assigned (`d = {"a": 1, "b": 2, "c": 3}`), both `visit_Assign -> _check_dict_creation` and `visit_Dict` fire. The `_check_dict_creation` method adds to `_dict_literals` (line 195), and then `visit_Dict` also fires via `generic_visit` in `visit_Assign` and adds the same literal again (line 373). This means every assigned dict literal is counted twice in `_dict_literals`.

Since `_dict_literals` is not used (see 7.3), this has no current impact, but if someone fixes the dual-parse issue, they'd need to deduplicate.

## Completed Fixes (Critical + High + Medium)

**Critical — previously dead/broken features:**
1. **Python extractor** — added `async def` support and populated `params` field on `FunctionInfo`
2. **TS extractor** — fixed catastrophic backtracking regex (`[^=]*=>` → `[^;{]*?=>`), populated `params`, added string-aware brace counting
3. **Signature variance detector** — now functional (was completely dead since params were never populated). Found 15 inconsistent signatures in the Reigh codebase on first run
4. **Move command** — added two-phase apply with full rollback on failure
5. **Fixer atomic writes** — temp+`os.replace` pattern in common.py
6. **tsc subprocess** — added `timeout=120`

**High severity:**
7. **Narrative purity** — `_compute_reminders` no longer mutates `state`; returns updated history
8. **Reminder persistence** — now actually saved to disk after narrative computation (was silently lost)
9. **Comment stripping** — new `_strip_ts_comments` helper that preserves strings (replaces `re.sub(r"//[^\n]*")`)
10. **confidence="info"** → "low" (was undefined in scoring weights)
11. **Params fixer** — `_is_param_context` no longer unconditionally returns True; regex more precise
12. **Duplicate constants** — `_EXCLUDED_ZONES` and `TIER_WEIGHTS` now imported from canonical sources

**Medium severity:**
13. **Naming detector** — added `snake_case` and `flat_lower` (was misclassifying as camelCase)
14. **Python except regex** — now handles tuple except clauses
15. **lru_cache** — `_extra_exclusions` passed as cache key parameter
16. **load_state** — catches `OSError`
17. **Phase detection** — no longer returns `early_momentum` for declining scores
18. **next.py** — fixed resolve hint argument order
19. **detect.py** — requires `--lang` flag instead of hardcoding typescript
20. **_ResultsWithMeta** — class-level mutable default → `__init__`

| #10 Dead code path detector | Functions always returning same value, unused default fields | `_is_param_context` always True, empty `params` lists |
| #11 Unsafe file write detector | `write_text()` without atomic pattern | All fixer non-atomic writes, move command |
| #12 Regex backtracking detector | Nested quantifiers, overlapping alternations | TS fn_re catastrophic backtracking |
| #13 Subprocess without timeout | `subprocess.run()` without `timeout=` | tsc hanging forever |
| #14 Mutable class default detector | `class Foo: data = {}` | `_ResultsWithMeta` shared dict |
| #15 Comment-stripping detector | `re.sub(r"//")` without string awareness | All naive comment stripping |
