## Deep Code Review: TypeScript Language Plugin

### 1. BUGS AND LOGIC ERRORS

**[BUG] `_detect_catch_return_default` shadows `lines` parameter (smells.py:950)**

In `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/detectors/smells.py`, line 950:

```python
def _detect_catch_return_default(filepath: str, content: str,
                                  smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]):
    ...
    if default_fields >= 2:
        line_no = content[:m.start()].count("\n") + 1
        lines = content.splitlines()  # <-- shadows nothing here, but wasteful
```

The function signature takes `content` but not `lines`. It calls `content.splitlines()` on every matching catch block, re-splitting the entire file content each time. This isn't a correctness bug per se, but it is wasteful. Worse, the outer function `detect_smells` has already split the content into `lines` but doesn't pass it here. This is inconsistent with all other multi-line helpers like `_detect_window_globals`, `_detect_swallowed_errors`, etc., which receive `lines` as a parameter.

**[BUG] `extract_ts_functions` never populates `params` (extractors.py:67)**

In `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/extractors.py`, the `FunctionInfo` objects are created without a `params` field:

```python
functions.append(FunctionInfo(
    name=name,
    file=filepath,
    line=start_line + 1,
    end_line=j + 1,
    loc=j - start_line + 1,
    body=body,
    normalized=normalized,
    body_hash=hashlib.md5(normalized.encode()).hexdigest(),
    # NO params= argument
))
```

Since `FunctionInfo.params` defaults to `[]`, the `detect_signature_variance` detector in `signature.py` will always see empty param lists for all TS functions, meaning `param_signatures` will always be `{()}` -- a set with one element (the empty tuple). The `len(param_signatures) < 2` guard will always be true, and **signature variance will never be detected for TypeScript**. The entire feature is silently broken for TS while it works for Python (which uses AST-based param extraction).

**[BUG] `hook_total` calculation excludes `custom_hooks` (extractors.py:134)**

In `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/extractors.py`:

```python
"hook_total": context_hooks + use_effects + use_states + use_refs,
```

But `custom_hooks` is counted separately via `use[A-Z]\w+\s*\(`. This regex *also matches* `useEffect`, `useState`, `useRef`, and `use*Context`, so `custom_hooks` double-counts the built-in hooks. Then `hook_total` excludes it to avoid double-counting. But the `TS_GOD_RULES` in `__init__.py` line 57 has a rule `GodRule("custom_hooks", ...)` with threshold 8, which would fire on the inflated number. The double-counting means `custom_hooks` will fire more easily than intended. The correct approach would be to subtract known hooks from `custom_hooks`: `custom_hooks = total_use_hooks - context_hooks - use_effects - use_states - use_refs`.

**[BUG] `_count_return_fields` depth tracking is wrong (react.py:251-273)**

In `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/detectors/react.py`, the function `_count_return_fields` checks `if depth == 1 and stripped.startswith("return")` after processing braces on the line. But depth is updated *during* the character scan, and by the time you check `stripped.startswith("return")`, depth reflects the state *after* the line, not *at the start of* the line. If a `return {` line itself opens a brace (changing depth from 1 to 2), the `depth == 1` check will fail, missing the return statement. This is a timing/ordering issue: depth should be checked *before* processing the line's braces, or the return-finding logic should be integrated into the character scan.

**[BUG] `detect_context_nesting` processes closing tags before opening tags (react.py:139-151)**

In `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/detectors/react.py`:

```python
for line in lines:
    # Count closing tags first
    for m in provider_close.finditer(line):
        if provider_stack and provider_stack[-1] == m.group(1):
            provider_stack.pop()
            depth -= 1
    # Count opening tags
    for m in provider_open.finditer(line):
        depth += 1
        provider_stack.append(m.group(1))
```

If a line has both `</FooProvider><BarProvider>`, the close is processed first (correct). But if a line has `<FooProvider></FooProvider>`, the close is processed first before the open, which means `provider_stack[-1]` won't match `FooProvider` (it hasn't been pushed yet), so the close is silently ignored. The open then pushes `FooProvider` and depth increments permanently. This leads to depth inflation for self-contained provider usage on one line.

### 2. FALSE POSITIVE / FALSE NEGATIVE RISKS

**`any_type` matches `: any` inside strings, JSX text, and type-narrowing comments (smells.py:26)**

The pattern `r":\s*any\b"` will match:
- Object keys like `anyObject: any` (correct) but also
- CSS properties in template literals: `` border: any; `` (technically caught by `_ts_match_is_in_string`, but only if the template literal is on a single line)
- More importantly: TypeScript's `as any` casts don't match (no colon), so they are false negatives
- The pattern also matches inside JSX text content: `<p>Type: anywhere</p>` would not match (word boundary), but `<p>value: any</p>` would

**`non_null_assert` pattern is over-simplified (smells.py:44)**

The pattern `r"\w+!\."` matches any word character followed by `!.`. This will false-positive on:
- Logical not followed by optional chaining: `!obj.prop` (though this is `!` then `obj.`, not `\w+!.`)
- Actually, the bigger false-positive concern is JSX exclamation in template literals: ``thing!.other`` is legitimate TS non-null assertion, so this is mostly correct. But it will miss `(expr)!.prop` since `\w+` won't match closing parens.

**`async_no_await` doesn't account for `.then()` chains (smells.py:393-428)**

An async function that returns a Promise via `.then()` chains instead of `await` is a style preference, not a bug. But this detector would flag it. Also, async functions that just wrap a return of another async call (`return fetch(...)`) without `await` are valid -- they just pass through the promise. These are false positives.

**`async_no_await` brace counting doesn't skip strings/comments (smells.py:411-413)**

```python
for ch in body_line:
    if ch == '{':
        brace_depth += 1
    elif ch == '}':
        brace_depth -= 1
```

Unlike the more careful `_track_brace_body` helper, this one counts braces inside strings and comments. A template literal containing `{` and `}` would throw off the depth tracking, potentially causing the function body extent to be wrong, leading to false negatives (premature termination) or false positives (scanning past the function).

**`hardcoded_url` false positives on test fixtures and API endpoint definitions (smells.py:96-98)**

The only URL constant exclusion is `^(?:export\s+)?(?:const|let|var)\s+[A-Z_][A-Z0-9_]*\s*=`. This misses:
- `const apiUrl = "https://..."` (camelCase constant)
- API endpoint definitions in config files
- URLs in JSDoc `@see` comments (though comments are partially filtered)

**`dead_exports` false positives from dynamic access (exports.py:60-68)**

The detector uses word-boundary grep to check if a symbol name appears in other files. But it can't detect:
- Dynamic imports: `import(modulePath).then(m => m[symbolName])`
- Computed property access on imported modules
- Symbols consumed by external packages that import from the library
- Re-exports via `export * from './module'`

**`detect_mixed_concerns` uses `return\s*\(?\s*<` which matches non-JSX (concerns.py:31)**

The pattern `return\s*\(?\s*<` would match:
- `return (a < b)` -- comparison operator in parenthesized return
- `return <T>value` -- generic cast in TS (angle bracket assertion)

This could cause false positives for non-component files that happen to use generic casts in returns and also fetch data.

**`detect_state_sync` only matches `useEffect\s*\(\s*\(\s*\)\s*=>\s*\{` (react.py:44)**

This misses:
- Effects with cleanup return: `useEffect(() => { setSomething(x); return () => cleanup(); }, [x])` -- wouldn't match as arrow-with-empty-params only
- Actually, the regex does require `() => {`, so it will match. But it won't match `useEffect(function() {` (rare but valid) or effects using named callbacks: `useEffect(handleChange, [dep])`

**`_compute_ts_destructure_props` matches non-component destructuring (__init__.py:22)**

The pattern `r"\{\s*(\w+(?:\s*,\s*\w+){8,})\s*\}"` matches ANY destructuring with 9+ items, not just component prop destructuring. This includes:
- `const { a, b, c, d, e, f, g, h, i } = someApiResponse` (data destructuring in utility functions)
- Object destructuring in test files
- Destructuring imports would not match (they use `{}` after `from`)

---

### 3. EDGE CASES

**Template literal `${...}` expressions containing braces break `_ts_match_is_in_string` (smells.py:275-311)**

The `_ts_match_is_in_string` function treats a backtick as toggling in/out of a string, but it has no awareness of `${}` interpolation depth. If a template literal contains `${obj.method()}`, the `}` after the call would be treated as ending the template literal prematurely:

```typescript
const x = `result: ${someFunc({ key: value })} done`;
//                              ^-- in_str toggles for { }
//                                         ^-- } doesn't end template
//                                                    ^-- ` ends template
```

Wait -- actually the function uses simple backtick toggling without `${}` awareness, so `${...}` content is simply scanned through character by character. Since `{` and `}` are not `'`, `"`, or `` ` ``, they are just skipped. The backtick-toggle approach *happens to work* for single-line template literals that don't contain nested backticks. But it will fail on nested template literals:

```typescript
const x = `outer ${`inner`} still outer`;
```

The inner backtick would prematurely close the outer template, causing the rest of the line to be treated as code.

**Generics with complex constraints break `_COMPONENT_PATTERNS` (extractors.py:145-156)**

```typescript
const MyComponent: React.FC<Props & { extra: string }> = ({ prop1, prop2 }) =>
```

The regex `(?:\s*:\s*React\.FC\w*<[^>]*>)?` uses `[^>]*` which cannot handle nested `>` in generics. The above would match `<Props & { extra: string` and then fail because the `>` after `string` closes the group. This is a known hard problem with regex-based parsing of generics.

Similarly, generics with conditional types or template literal types would break:
```typescript
const Comp: React.FC<Extract<AllProps, { type: "foo" }>> = ({ prop1 }) =>
```

**Decorators on arrow functions (smells.py:796-798)**

The dead function detector skips decorated functions:
```python
if i > 0 and lines[i - 1].strip().startswith("@"):
    continue
```

But TypeScript decorators can be multi-line or have multiple decorators. Only checking `i-1` means:
```typescript
@injectable()
@singleton()
const myService = () => {
    return null;
}
```
Only the `@singleton()` line is checked, so this works by accident. But if there's a blank line between decorator and function, it won't skip.

**Enum declarations confuse the prop counter (props.py:19-21)**

The `_BLOAT_SUFFIXES` regex `r"(?:Props|Context|ContextValue|ContextType|State|StateValue)\w*"` combined with `(?:export\s+)?(?:interface|type)\s+` would not match enums (which are excluded by the keyword match). But the property counter counts `\n` at `brace_depth == 1` followed by a non-empty, non-comment line. This is fine for interfaces but would miscount for type aliases that use union types:

```typescript
type ContextState = {
    value1: string;
    value2: number;
} | {
    value3: string; // counted as prop despite being in a separate union branch
};
```

**Namespace declarations and declaration merging**

None of the detectors account for TypeScript namespaces or declaration merging. An interface declared across multiple `declare module` blocks would have its properties undercounted by the prop detector (which only finds the first `{` block). Similarly, export detection doesn't handle `namespace` exports.

---

### 4. INCONSISTENCIES

**Python uses AST for monster/dead function detection; TS uses regex brace-tracking**

The Python plugin's `_detect_monster_functions` and `_detect_dead_functions` use Python's `ast` module for precise, correct function boundary detection. The TS plugin uses regex + brace counting, which is inherently fragile (as documented above with string/comment/template literal edge cases). This is an understandable trade-off (no TS AST available in Python), but it means the TS plugin has lower detection accuracy.

**Python's `_match_is_in_string` handles triple-quotes; TS's `_ts_match_is_in_string` doesn't handle `${}` nesting**

The Python version handles `'''` and `"""` string literals with proper nesting. The TS version handles `'`, `"`, and `` ` `` but treats backtick-delimited strings as simple toggle-on/toggle-off, with no `${}` interpolation tracking. The `_build_ts_line_state` function *does* handle `${}` properly for multi-line template literals, creating an inconsistency: the line-level classifier is more sophisticated than the inline match checker.

**TS smells compile regex per-line instead of pre-compiling (smells.py:344)**

```python
for i, line in enumerate(lines):
    ...
    m = re.search(check["pattern"], line)
```

Python's `re` module caches compiled patterns, so this isn't catastrophic, but it is inconsistent with the explicit `re.compile` calls used elsewhere in the same file and throughout the Python plugin.

**TS `_detect_error_no_throw` uses a 3-line lookahead; Python uses full except-block walking**

The TS version (smells.py:431-442) just looks at the 3 lines after `console.error` for `throw`/`return`. The Python version walks the entire except block body. A TS function could have `console.error(...)` followed by 4 lines of cleanup before `throw`, which would be falsely flagged.

**`detect_deprecated` uses `SRC_PATH` rather than the given `path` for importer counting (deprecated.py:110)**

```python
def _count_importers(name: str, declaring_file: str) -> int:
    ...
    ts_files = find_ts_files(SRC_PATH)  # Always searches from SRC_PATH
```

This hardcodes the search scope to `SRC_PATH` regardless of what `--path` was passed. The Python plugin doesn't have an equivalent detector, but this is inconsistent with other TS detectors that respect the path parameter for scanning but use `SRC_PATH` for reference checking (like `exports.py`). In `deprecated.py`, this is actually intentional (checking all importers project-wide), but it should be documented.

---

### 5. REGEX QUALITY

**Potential catastrophic backtracking in `fn_re` (extractors.py:28-34)**

```python
fn_re = re.compile(
    r"^(?:export\s+)?(?:"
    r"(?:function\s+(\w+))|"
    r"(?:const\s+(\w+)\s*(?::\s*[^=]+)?\s*=\s*(?:\([^)]*\)|[^=])*=>)|"
    r"(?:const\s+(\w+)\s*(?::\s*[^=]+)?\s*=\s*function)"
    r")"
)
```

The fragment `(?:\([^)]*\)|[^=])*=>` is dangerous. `[^=]` matches nearly anything, and the alternation with `\([^)]*\)` means the engine can partition the input in exponentially many ways. On a long line like `const x: SomeType<Foo, Bar> = someValue =>` where `=>` doesn't appear, the regex engine will backtrack extensively.

For a line like:
```typescript
const foo: VeryLong<Generic<Type<With<Many<Levels>>>>> = (param1, param2) => {
```

The `[^=]+` in `(?::\s*[^=]+)?` will greedily consume everything including the `=` in `=>`, then backtrack character by character. Combined with the outer `(?:\([^)]*\)|[^=])*`, this creates a multiplicative backtracking scenario.

**`nested_ternaries` regex is imprecise (__init__.py:48-49)**

```python
ComplexitySignal("nested ternaries", r"[^?]\?[^?.:\n][^:\n]*[^?]\?[^?.]",
                 weight=3, threshold=2),
```

This attempts to find `? ... ? ...` on a single line, but:
- It doesn't distinguish ternary `?` from optional chaining `?.` (the `[^?.]` at the end tries but is incomplete)
- It doesn't account for nullish coalescing `??`
- The `[^?\n]` in the middle allows matching across virtually anything on the line
- It would match legitimate patterns like `type?.value ? x : y` (optional chaining followed by ternary)

**`voided_symbol` regex may false-positive on `void` type annotations (smells.py:129)**

```python
r"^\s*void\s+[a-zA-Z_]\w*\s*;?\s*$"
```

This would match `void someFunction;` as a dead code smell, but it would also match if someone writes a type annotation on its own line (unlikely but possible). More concerning: it misses `void expression` patterns that are multi-line or have more complex expressions like `void someObj.method()`.

**`debug_tag` pattern matches JSX string attributes (smells.py:108)**

```python
r"""(?:['"`])\[([A-Z][A-Z0-9_]{2,})\]\s"""
```

This would match JSX attributes like `label="[ERROR] Something"` which might be intentional UI text, not debug tags. The `_ts_match_is_in_string` check won't help here because the match starts at the quote character itself.

### 6. PERFORMANCE

**`_detect_catch_return_default` re-splits content on every catch match (smells.py:950)**

```python
lines = content.splitlines()  # called inside the loop, once per matching catch block
```

This should be done once outside the loop, or better, the `lines` parameter should be passed in like other helpers.

**`catch_re` is compiled inside `_detect_swallowed_errors` and `_detect_catch_return_default` (smells.py:620, 881)**

Both functions compile the same `catch_re = re.compile(r"catch\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{")` pattern. While Python caches compiled patterns, defining it once at module level or as a shared constant would be cleaner.

**`detect_smells` runs `re.search(check["pattern"], line)` for every check on every line (smells.py:344)**

There are ~13 regex-based checks. For a codebase with 1000 files and 200 lines each, that's 2.6 million regex operations. Pre-compiling the patterns and storing them on the check dicts would help, and potentially combining compatible patterns with alternation could reduce passes.

**`_count_importers` in deprecated.py scans ALL TS files for each deprecated symbol (deprecated.py:106-117)**

Each call to `_count_importers` runs `find_ts_files(SRC_PATH)` (which walks the filesystem) and then `grep_count_files`. For N deprecated symbols, this is N full filesystem scans + N full-text searches. The exports detector solves this correctly with a batch reference index (`_build_reference_index`). The deprecated detector should use the same approach.

**`extract_ts_functions` brace counting doesn't skip strings (extractors.py:52-53)**

```python
for ch in lines[j]:
    if ch == '{':
        brace_depth += 1
    elif ch == '}':
        brace_depth -= 1
```

This is the same issue as `_detect_async_no_await` -- braces inside strings or template literals will throw off the tracking. In a file with many template literals containing `{...}`, this could cause the function extractor to identify wrong boundaries, leading to incorrect `body` and `body_hash` values for the duplicate detector.

**`detect_dead_exports` runs `_build_reference_index` on ALL unique export names at once (exports.py:57-59)**

This is actually well-designed for batching, but `grep_files_containing` may itself be doing per-name scans internally. If it does N separate grep passes for N names, the complexity is O(N * files). Worth verifying that `grep_files_containing` is batch-optimized.

### 7. DEAD CODE / UNUSED IMPORTS

**`sys` imported but only used in `cmd_exports` display (exports.py:3)**

`sys` is imported at the top of `exports.py` and used for `print(..., file=sys.stderr)` in `cmd_exports` and the inline progress message. Not dead code, just a minor observation.

**`defaultdict` imported in logs.py but also used -- no dead code**

After thorough review, I found no truly dead imports across the TS plugin files. All imports appear to be used.

**`MAX_FUNC_SCAN` and `MAX_EFFECT_BODY` are defined but could be unified**

`MAX_EFFECT_BODY = 1000` (react.py:9) and `MAX_FUNC_SCAN = 2000` (react.py:10) serve similar purposes (capping how far to scan for brace matching) but have different values and different semantics (characters vs lines). This isn't dead code but is a design inconsistency.

### ADDITIONAL FINDINGS

**The `_build_ts_line_state` template literal tracker has a subtle bug with backticks inside `${}` (smells.py:175-198)**

When in a template literal and scanning for `${` expressions, the code correctly tracks `template_brace_depth`. But what if a `${}` expression contains another template literal?

```typescript
const x = `outer ${`inner ${value}`} still outer`;
```

Line 192: `if ch == "`" and template_brace_depth == 0: in_template = False`. But when we're inside `${...}` (depth > 0), encountering a backtick starts a *nested* template literal, which this code doesn't handle. The inner backtick would be skipped, and the closing backtick of the inner template would also be skipped (since depth > 0). Then the `}` would decrement depth. Then the final backtick would close the outer template. In this specific case, it happens to work by accident because the inner template's backticks are consumed as regular characters. But it would fail on:

```typescript
const x = `outer ${fn(`inner`)} still outer`;
```

Here the inner `` `inner` `` has its own opening and closing backtick. The first backtick is at depth 1, so it's skipped. The second backtick is also at depth 1, also skipped. This works. But add nesting:

```typescript
const x = `a ${fn(`b ${c}`)} d`;
```

The state machine sees: depth=0, backtick (enter template), `$`, `{` (depth=1), backtick (skip, depth>0), `$`, `{` (depth=2), `}` (depth=1), backtick (skip, depth>0), `}` (depth=0), then next backtick would close the outer template. This actually works correctly by accident because `${}` depth tracking causes inner backticks to be "absorbed." However, this is fragile -- it works for the right reasons in most cases but would break on unbalanced content within `${}`.

**`_find_function_start` may match class methods (smells.py:703-753)**

The function is designed for top-level functions but would also match methods if they happen to be on their own line with proper patterns. However, since it checks for `const|let|var` or `function` keywords, class method syntax (`methodName() {`) would not match. Class methods using arrow functions as properties (`methodName = () => {`) inside a class body would also not match because they lack the `const` keyword. So this is likely fine.

**`provider_open` regex doesn't handle JSX spread or complex attributes (react.py:121)**

```python
provider_open = re.compile(r"<(\w+Provider)\b(?!.*/>)")  # opening, not self-closing
```

The negative lookahead `(?!.*/>)` checks if there's a `/>` anywhere later on the line. But multi-line JSX is common:

```tsx
<SomeProvider
  value={value}
>
```

Here the opening tag spans multiple lines, and the `/>` check on line 1 would correctly not find it, but on a line like `<SomeProvider value={fn()} />` it works correctly. However, the regex would match self-closing providers that have `/>` on a *different* line:

```tsx
<SomeProvider
  value={val}
/>
```

Line 1: `<SomeProvider` -- no `/>` on this line, so it matches as an opening tag. But it's actually self-closing. This would increment depth incorrectly, with no matching close, inflating the nesting count.

---

### SUMMARY OF CRITICAL ISSUES (ranked by impact)

1. **`extract_ts_functions` never sets `params`**: Signature variance detection is completely non-functional for TypeScript.

2. **Brace counting in strings/template literals**: `extract_ts_functions` and `_detect_async_no_await` count braces inside strings, causing incorrect function boundary detection. This is a systemic issue affecting function extraction accuracy, duplicate detection, and async-no-await smell detection.

3. **`_count_importers` in deprecated.py**: O(N * filesystem) performance -- should batch like exports.py does.

4. **`custom_hooks` double-counting**: The custom hooks metric includes built-in hooks, inflating the count used by the god component rules.

5. **`_count_return_fields` depth check timing**: The depth is checked after line processing, potentially missing `return {` statements at the correct depth.

6. **`fn_re` backtracking risk**: The function extraction regex has alternations with `[^=]*` that could cause pathological backtracking on complex type annotations.

7. **`_ts_match_is_in_string` lacks `${}` handling**: Unlike `_build_ts_line_state`, the inline string checker treats backtick strings as simple toggles, creating inconsistency in string detection accuracy between line-level and match-level checks.</result>

Here is the complete data model consistency audit for `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/`. I have traced findings from creation through storage through consumption and identified the following issues, ordered by severity.

---

## Critical Issues

### 1. `confidence="info"` is not handled by scoring or display systems
- **File**: `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/scan.py`, line 43
- **Issue**: `_audit_excluded_dirs` creates findings with `confidence="info"`, but this value does not exist in `CONFIDENCE_WEIGHTS` (`scoring.py:35`) or `CONFIDENCE_ORDER` (`plan.py:23`). Both define only `{"high", "medium", "low"}`.
- **Effect**: When scoring encounters a `stale_exclude` finding, `CONFIDENCE_WEIGHTS.get(f.get("confidence", "medium"), 0.7)` falls back to 0.7 (the default), treating `"info"` as medium. In `CONFIDENCE_ORDER`, `.get("info", 9)` sorts it to the very bottom (sort value 9). The confidence badge `[info]` displays in show/plan output but is undefined visually.
- **Severity**: **HIGH** -- silent scoring miscalculation for these findings.

### 2. `extract_functions` type annotation says `list[dict]` but returns `list[FunctionInfo]`
- **File**: `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/base.py`, line 76
- **Issue**: The `LangConfig.extract_functions` field is annotated as `Callable[[str], list[dict]] | None`, but both `_ts_extract_functions` (TS `__init__.py:463`) and `_py_extract_functions` (Python `__init__.py:266`) return `list[FunctionInfo]` (dataclass instances). The signature type also says `str` argument but callers pass `Path`.
- **Effect**: Downstream consumers must use `getattr(f, "file", "")` (as in `signature.py:52` and `base.py:336`) instead of `f["file"]`. If anyone writes new code trusting the annotation and uses dict access, it will crash at runtime with `TypeError: 'FunctionInfo' object is not subscriptable`.
- **Severity**: **HIGH** -- incorrect type annotation creates a trap for future code.

### 3. `stale_exclude` detector is not in any scoring Dimension
- **File**: `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/scoring.py`, lines 20-32 (DIMENSIONS list)
- **Issue**: The `stale_exclude` detector (created in `commands/scan.py:42`) is not listed in any `Dimension` in `scoring.py`. It is also not in `DETECTOR_TOOLS` in `narrative.py`.
- **Effect**: Findings from this detector are stored in state and visible in `show`, but they contribute zero to objective dimension scores (silently ignored by `_detector_pass_rate`). They still affect the legacy weighted-progress score (`_weighted_progress` in `state.py`). Additionally, `_count_open_by_detector` in narrative counts them, but `_compute_actions` won't generate any action for them since they have no entry in `DETECTOR_TOOLS`.
- **Severity**: **MEDIUM** -- orphaned detector findings with inconsistent scoring treatment.

---

## High-Severity Issues

### 4. Duplicate `_EXCLUDED_ZONES` definitions that could drift
- **Files**: 
  - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/scoring.py`, line 46: `_EXCLUDED_ZONES = {"test", "config", "generated", "vendor"}`
  - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/zones.py`, line 25: `EXCLUDED_ZONES = {Zone.TEST, Zone.CONFIG, Zone.GENERATED, Zone.VENDOR}`
  - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/zones.py`, line 28: `EXCLUDED_ZONE_VALUES = {z.value for z in EXCLUDED_ZONES}`
- **Issue**: `scoring.py` defines its own hardcoded set of excluded zone strings rather than importing `EXCLUDED_ZONE_VALUES` from `zones.py`. If a new zone is added to `zones.py` (say `Zone.DRAFT`), `scoring.py` would not exclude it.
- **Severity**: **HIGH** -- violation of single source of truth principle for a critical scoring parameter.

### 5. `_compute_fp_rates` returns `dict[tuple[str, str], float]` -- not JSON-serializable
- **File**: `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/narrative.py`, line 646
- **Issue**: The function returns a dict with `tuple` keys `(detector, zone)`. While the return value is only consumed internally within `_compute_reminders` (line 765) and never directly serialized, the pattern is fragile. If this dict were ever included in the narrative return value or state, JSON serialization would fail because tuple keys are not valid JSON.
- **Severity**: **LOW** -- currently contained but architecturally fragile.

### 6. `debt_review` actions have a different schema than other action types
- **File**: `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/narrative.py`, lines 456-462
- **Issue**: The `debt_review` action dict has keys `{priority, type, description, command, gap}` while all other action types have `{priority, type, count, description, command, impact, dimension}`. The `count`, `impact`, and `dimension` keys are missing from `debt_review`. Code in `narrative.py:689` accesses `a.get("count", 0)` (safe due to `.get`), and `narrative.py:466` accesses `a.get("impact", 0)` for sorting (safe), but any consumer expecting a uniform action schema will be surprised.
- **Severity**: **MEDIUM** -- schema inconsistency that makes actions heterogeneous.

---

## Medium-Severity Issues

### 7. `_empty_state()` is missing many keys that `merge_scan` adds
- **File**: `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/state.py`, lines 23-33 vs 337-376
- **Issue**: `_empty_state()` returns `{version, created, last_scan, scan_count, config, score, stats, findings}`, but after a scan, state accumulates: `tool_hash`, `potentials`, `codebase_metrics`, `scan_completeness`, `scan_history`, `dimension_scores`, `objective_score`, `objective_strict`, `strict_score`, `zone_distribution`, `reminder_history`. All callers use `.get()` or `.setdefault()` so this doesn't crash, but the initial schema definition is misleading and there's no schema validation.
- **Severity**: **MEDIUM** -- data model documentation mismatch. Any code that tries to validate state against `_empty_state()` would reject valid state files.

### 8. `STRUCTURAL_MERGE` excludes `structural` itself, creating split behavior
- **File**: `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/narrative.py`, line 54
- **Issue**: `STRUCTURAL_MERGE = {"large", "complexity", "gods", "concerns"}` -- these are the sub-detector names that get merged into `"structural"`. But `merge_structural_signals` in `base.py:197` already creates findings with `detector="structural"`. So the merge set only matters for display/grouping in narrative, and the detectors `large`, `complexity`, `gods`, `concerns` never appear as finding detector names in practice. The set exists to handle legacy data where these names *were* used as detector names. This is fine but undocumented.
- **Severity**: **LOW** -- defensive code that handles a case that may no longer occur.

### 9. `_write_query` uses `default=str` while `save_state` uses `_json_default`
- **Files**:
  - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/cli.py`, line 21: `json.dumps(data, indent=2, default=str)`
  - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/state.py`, line 89: `json.dumps(state, indent=2, default=_json_default)`
- **Issue**: Two different JSON serialization strategies. `default=str` silently converts any non-serializable object to its string representation (e.g., a `Path` becomes `PosixPath('/...')` instead of a clean path string, a `set` becomes `{...}` string). The `_json_default` in `state.py` properly converts `set` to sorted list and `Path` to `str()`. If query data contains a `Path` object, it gets `PosixPath(...)` instead of a clean path.
- **Severity**: **MEDIUM** -- query.json could contain garbled representations of non-standard types.

### 10. `scan_history` entries missing `lang` field on pre-language-scoping data
- **File**: `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/narrative.py`, lines 83-87
- **Issue**: `compute_narrative` filters history by `h.get("lang") in (lang, None)`, intentionally including entries without a `lang` field for backward compatibility. But this means mixing Python and TypeScript history entries from before the language-scoping feature was added. If a user scans both languages, pre-scoping history pollutes both language trajectories.
- **Severity**: **LOW** -- only affects projects that existed before the language-scoping feature AND scan multiple languages.

### 11. `_analyze_debt` computes `overall_lenient`/`overall_strict` without sample dampening
- **File**: `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/narrative.py`, lines 265-282
- **Issue**: `_analyze_debt` computes a tier-weighted average of dimension scores (lines 267-282) but does not apply the `MIN_SAMPLE` dampening that `compute_objective_score` uses (scoring.py:178). This means the debt gap calculation can be dominated by small-sample dimensions, making it inconsistent with the official objective score.
- **Severity**: **LOW** -- the debt analysis is informational and doesn't feed into the official score.

---

## Low-Severity Issues

### 12. `"zone"` field on findings is a string (enum `.value`), not a `Zone` enum
- **File**: `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/plan.py`, line 85: `f["zone"] = zone.value`
- **Issue**: Zone is stored as a string (`"production"`, `"test"`, etc.) on findings. This is consistent and correct for JSON serialization. # Regex Audit Report: desloppify codebase

## CRITICAL ISSUES

### Issue 1: Catastrophic Backtracking in TS Function Extractor
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/extractors.py`, line 28-33
**Pattern:**
```python
fn_re = re.compile(
    r"^(?:export\s+)?(?:"
    r"(?:function\s+(\w+))|"
    r"(?:const\s+(\w+)\s*(?::\s*[^=]+)?\s*=\s*(?:\([^)]*\)|[^=])*=>)|"
    r"(?:const\s+(\w+)\s*(?::\s*[^=]+)?\s*=\s*function)"
    r")"
)
```
**Problem:** The second alternative contains `(?:\([^)]*\)|[^=])*=>`. The character class `[^=]` matches anything except `=`, and the alternation `\([^)]*\)|[^=]` creates overlapping matches. On a line like `const foo: SomeType = someValue` that does NOT end with `=>`, the engine must try every possible partition of the text between `\([^)]*\)` and `[^=]` before backtracking fails. With a long type annotation or right-hand side, this is **O(2^n)** in the worst case.

Specifically, `([^)]*\)|[^=])*` -- the `[^=]` can match the same characters that `\([^)]*\)` matches outside the parens, creating catastrophic backtracking when the overall match fails (no `=>` at the end).

**Severity:** HIGH -- this pattern runs on every line of every TS/TSX file during function extraction. A crafted or naturally long line (e.g., `const foo: VeryLongGenericType<A, B, C, D, E, F> = something`) without `=>` will cause exponential runtime.

**Fix:** Use an atomic group or possessive quantifier equivalent, or restructure to avoid the nested quantifier. For example, match `=\s*` then branch: `(?:async\s+)?(?:\([^)]*\)\s*=>|function)`.

---

### Issue 2: Catastrophic Backtracking in Nested Ternary Detection
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/__init__.py`, line 48
**Pattern:** `r"[^?]\?[^?.:\n][^:\n]*[^?]\?[^?.]"`
**Problem:** The segment `[^?.:\n][^:\n]*[^?]` contains adjacent character classes with overlapping ranges. `[^?.:\n]` and `[^:\n]*` overlap on most characters. On a line with lots of content between two `?` characters that does NOT have a second `?`, the engine will try every partition between the first and second character class before failing. This is classic quadratic backtracking (O(n^2) for a line of length n).

**Severity:** MEDIUM -- it's used via `re.findall` on full file contents, so a long line with a single `?` and no colons could be slow, but the practical worst-case inputs are uncommon.

**Fix:** Make `[^:\n]*` possessive or atomic, or use a simpler non-overlapping structure.

---

### Issue 3: Catastrophic Backtracking in Nested Comprehension Detection
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/__init__.py`, line 42-43
**Pattern:** `r"\[.*\bfor\b.*\bfor\b.*\]|\{.*\bfor\b.*\bfor\b.*\}"`
**Problem:** `.*` is greedy and unbounded. On a line containing `[` but no second `for` before `]`, the engine must try every possible split of `.*\bfor\b.*\bfor\b.*` before failing. With three greedy `.*` segments, this is **O(n^3)** per line -- cubic time on content length.

**Severity:** MEDIUM -- the pattern is applied via `re.findall` on full file content with `re.MULTILINE`, but `.` won't match `\n` by default, so it's bounded per-line. Still, long lines with `[` and one `for` will cause quadratic behavior.

**Fix:** Use lazy quantifiers `.*?` or better, a negative character class `[^\]]*` / `[^\}]*` to bound the search.

---

## HIGH SEVERITY ISSUES

### Issue 4: `[^)]*` in Multi-line Context is Broken
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/detectors/complexity.py`, line 11
**Pattern:** `re.compile(r"def\s+\w+\s*\(([^)]*)\)", re.DOTALL)`
**Problem:** With `re.DOTALL`, `[^)]*` will match across lines until the FIRST `)` in the file after the `(`. For Python function signatures that use nested parentheses in type annotations like `def foo(bar: Callable[..., tuple[int, ...]])`, this will match incorrectly -- the `[^)]*` stops at the first `)` inside the annotation, not the signature-closing `)`.

**Severity:** HIGH -- this causes incorrect parameter counting for any function with nested parentheses in its signature.

**Fix:** Either track parenthesis depth manually, or remove the `re.DOTALL` flag and handle multi-line signatures differently.

---

### Issue 5: Python Func Pattern Eats Too Much with DOTALL
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/extractors.py`, line 249-251
**Pattern:**
```python
_PY_FUNC_PATTERN = re.compile(
    r"^def\s+(\w+)\s*\(([^)]*)\)\s*(?:->.*?)?:",
    re.MULTILINE | re.DOTALL,
)
```
**Problem:** Same issue as Issue 4 -- `[^)]*` with DOTALL stops at the first `)` in the file, not the matching one. Also, `(?:->.*?)?:` with DOTALL will match across lines: `->` followed by anything (including newlines) lazily until `:`. This could span multiple function definitions if the return type annotation is followed by newlines before `:`.

**Severity:** HIGH -- causes incorrect function boundary detection. A function like `def foo(x: tuple[int, int]) -> dict[str, Any]:` will be split at the first `)` rather than the signature-closing one.

---

### Issue 6: `non_null_assert` Pattern False Positives
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/detectors/smells.py`, line 42
**Pattern:** `r"\w+!\."`
**Problem:** This matches ANY word character followed by `!.`, including negation in comparisons like `foo !== bar` where `!.` is `!=` followed by `=`. Wait -- actually `!.` is `!` followed by `.`, which is different. But the pattern will false-positive on string contents like `"hello!.world"`, template literals like `` `${thing!.prop}` ``, and TypeScript assertion functions. However, the main issue is that it will match inside strings. The code does use `_ts_match_is_in_string` to filter this, so string false positives are handled.

Actually, the real false positive concern is `word!.` in destructuring or conditional chaining negation contexts. The pattern is broadly correct for non-null assertions. However, it will match `RegExp!.test` inside type assertion code that is actually intentional/correct.

**Severity:** LOW -- the string filtering helps, but the pattern is too simplistic for precise detection.

---

### Issue 7: `any_type` Pattern False Positives
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/detectors/smells.py`, line 24
**Pattern:** `r":\s*any\b"`
**Problem:** This will match `company: "any old string"` or variable names like `company: anyVariable`. The `\b` after `any` prevents matching `anything` but doesn't prevent matching `any` as a value in a different context. More importantly, it matches inside comments and strings. The code does use `_ts_match_is_in_string` for filtering, but the pattern will also match in JSDoc `@param {any}` or in type annotations that are intentionally `any` for framework compatibility.

**Severity:** LOW -- the string/comment filtering mitigates most false positives.

---

### Issue 8: `catch\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{` Matches Inside Strings/Comments
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/detectors/smells.py`, lines 18, 620, 881 (also `_smell_helpers.py` lines 239, 500)
**Pattern:** `r"catch\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{"`
**Problem:** When used via `catch_re.finditer(content)` (lines 621, 882, and in _smell_helpers.py), the regex is applied to the FULL file content, not line-by-line. There is no string/comment filtering for this whole-content search. A `catch` keyword inside a multi-line string, template literal, or comment would be falsely matched.

The brace-tracking body extraction code handles string escapes within the body, but the initial `catch\s*\(` match itself is not protected.

**Severity:** HIGH -- this runs on user codebases. A template literal like `` `try { } catch (e) { }` `` in a test helper string would be a false positive that could cause the detector to extract a nonsensical "body" from the subsequent real code.

---

### Issue 9: Comment Stripping Regex Matches Inside Strings
**File:** Multiple locations:
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/detectors/smells.py`, lines 601-602, 649-650, 826-827
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/detectors/_smell_helpers.py`, lines 220-221, 268-269, 445-446
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/detectors/react.py`, lines 75-76

**Patterns:**
```python
body_stripped = re.sub(r"//[^\n]*", "", body)
body_stripped = re.sub(r"/\*.*?\*/", "", body_stripped, flags=re.DOTALL)
```
**Problem:** These patterns strip `//` and `/* */` comments from code bodies, but they will also strip content from string literals that contain `//` or `/*`. For example, `const url = "https://example.com"` would have `//example.com"` removed, corrupting the analysis.

This is used to determine whether catch blocks, useEffect bodies, etc. are "empty" after comment removal. A string containing `//` would be partially stripped, potentially causing false positives (declaring code "empty" when it's not) or false negatives.

**Severity:** HIGH -- common in real-world TS code (URLs in strings, regex literals containing `//`, template literals with paths).

---

## MEDIUM SEVERITY ISSUES

### Issue 10: `mutable_default` Pattern Doesn't Handle Multiline Signatures
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/detectors/smells.py`, line 21
**Pattern:** `r"def\s+\w+\([^)]*=\s*(?:\[\]|\{\}|set\(\))"`
**Problem:** `[^)]*` will not span lines (since DOTALL is not used and this is applied per-line). A multi-line function signature with a mutable default on a different line than `def` will NOT be detected:
```python
def foo(
    bar=[],  # Not detected — 'def' is on a different line
):
```
**Severity:** MEDIUM -- many Python functions with many parameters will use multi-line signatures, and these are exactly the ones most likely to have mutable defaults.

---

### Issue 11: `hardcoded_url` Pattern Greedy Issue
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/detectors/smells.py`, line 96
**Pattern:** `r"""(?:['\"])https?://[^\s'\"]+(?:['\"])"""`
**Problem:** The opening quote `['\"]` and closing quote `['\"]` don't need to match. The pattern would match `'https://example.com"` (mismatched quotes). More importantly, `[^\s'\"]+` is greedy and will consume everything up to the last non-whitespace, non-quote character, which could span multiple URLs on the same line.

**Severity:** MEDIUM -- mismatched quotes won't occur in valid code, but the greedy quantifier could cause multi-URL lines to be counted as a single match.

---

### Issue 12: Regex Compiled Inside Function Body (Performance)
**Files:**
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/detectors/smells.py`, line 401: `async_re = re.compile(...)` inside `_detect_async_no_await`
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/detectors/smells.py`, line 620: `catch_re = re.compile(...)` inside `_detect_swallowed_errors`
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/detectors/smells.py`, line 849: `window_re = re.compile(...)` inside `_detect_window_globals`
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/detectors/smells.py`, line 881: `catch_re = re.compile(...)` inside `_detect_catch_return_default`
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/detectors/_smell_helpers.py`, lines 20, 239, 468, 500 (duplicates)
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/detectors/react.py`, line 44: `effect_re = re.compile(...)` inside loop
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/detectors/react.py`, lines 121-122: `provider_open`, `provider_close` inside function
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/detectors/react.py`, line 171: `hook_re` inside function
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/fixers/logs.py`, line 142: `re.compile(...)` inside loop

**Problem:** These `re.compile()` calls are inside functions or loops that are called once per file. While Python's `re` module has an internal cache (maxsize=512), the intent should be to compile once at module level.

The worst case is in `fixers/logs.py` line 142: `pattern = re.compile(r"\b" + re.escape(ident) + r"\b")` -- this is inside a nested loop (per-file, per-identifier), creating a new compiled pattern for each identifier. With many identifiers, this could thrash the re cache.

**Severity:** MEDIUM -- performance impact. `re.compile()` is internally cached, but the cache has limited size and these patterns are compiled repeatedly.

---

### Issue 13: Duplicate Code -- smells.py and _smell_helpers.py are Identical
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/detectors/smells.py` and `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/detectors/_smell_helpers.py`
**Problem:** The `_smell_helpers.py` file contains all the same multi-line detector functions that are already defined in `smells.py` (lines 393-956 in smells.py match lines 12-575 in _smell_helpers.py). The docstring says "Extracted from smells.py to keep file sizes manageable" and it imports from smells.py, but both files contain the same function definitions. This means the same regex patterns exist twice, and any bug fix applied to one location could be missed in the other.

**Severity:** MEDIUM -- maintenance risk. If patterns are fixed in one file but not the other, inconsistent behavior results.

---

### Issue 14: `except\s*(?:\w|:)` Incorrect Pattern
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/detectors/smells.py`, line 215
**Pattern:** `r"except\s*(?:\w|:)"`
**Problem:** This is used as a negative filter: `if not re.match(r"except\s*(?:\w|:)", stripped) and stripped != "except:"`. The character class `(?:\w|:)` matches a single word character or `:`. This means `except:` matches (via `:`) and `except SomeException` matches (via `\w`). But `except (TypeError, ValueError)` would NOT match because `(` is not `\w` or `:`. This means `except (ExceptionTuple)` blocks are silently skipped by `_walk_except_blocks`.

**Severity:** MEDIUM -- any `except (ExceptionA, ExceptionB) as e:` clause won't be analyzed for empty/swallowed error patterns.

**Fix:** Change to `r"except\s*(?:\w|\(|:)"` to also match tuple exception handling.

---

### Issue 15: `from\s+['"]` Doesn't Account for Inline Comments
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/detectors/deps.py`, line 19
**Pattern:** `r"from\s+['\"]"` (used as grep pattern)
**Problem:** This would match `from '` inside a comment like `// import X from 'y'` or inside a string literal. The grep_files function does line-by-line searching without comment/string awareness.

**Severity:** MEDIUM -- would create phantom dependency graph edges from commented-out import statements. Common during refactoring when old imports are commented out.

---

### Issue 16: Escape Sequence Checking Bug in Brace Trackers
**Files:** Multiple brace-tracking functions in smells.py, _smell_helpers.py, react.py
**Pattern:** `if ch == in_str and prev_ch != "\\":` (e.g., smells.py lines 543, 582, 630, etc.)
**Problem:** The escape check `prev_ch != "\\"` only handles a single backslash. A double-escaped string like `"foo\\\""` would be incorrect: the `\\` is an escaped backslash, and the `\"` is an escaped quote, so the string should NOT end at the `"` after `\\\"`. But `prev_ch` would be `\`, and the check would treat the `"` as escaped, when actually it's `\\` (literal backslash) followed by `\"` (escaped quote). The correct check needs to count consecutive backslashes.

This same bug exists in every brace-tracking body extraction function across the codebase.

**Severity:** MEDIUM -- the practical impact is limited since consecutive backslashes before quotes are rare in TS/JSX code, but it's a correctness bug that could cause the brace tracker to lose sync and extract incorrect function bodies.

---

### Issue 17: `_LOG_RE` Pattern Missing Anchoring
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/detectors/smells.py`, line 253
**Pattern:** `r"(?:print|logging\.\w+|logger\.\w+|log\.\w+)\s*\("`
**Problem:** Used as `re.match(...)` which anchors to the start of the string. But it's applied to `body_lines` which are stripped. If a line starts with code before the log call (e.g., `result = logger.info(...)` or `if cond: print(...)`), it won't match, and the swallowed-error detector will correctly identify non-logging lines. So this is actually correct behavior -- the issue is minor.

**Severity:** LOW -- the match anchoring is intentional for this use case.

## LOW SEVERITY ISSUES

### Issue 18: `\{\s*(\w+(?:\s*,\s*\w+){8,})\s*\}` Doesn't Handle Type Annotations
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/__init__.py`, line 22
**Pattern:** `r"\{\s*(\w+(?:\s*,\s*\w+){8,})\s*\}"`
**Problem:** This detects destructuring with 9+ props, but it won't match when destructured props have type annotations: `{ a: string, b: number, ... }`. The `:` breaks the `\w+` match. It also won't match props with defaults: `{ a = 5, b, c }`.

**Severity:** LOW -- this is a complexity signal heuristic; partial detection is acceptable for scoring.

### Issue 19: `_COMPONENT_PATTERNS` Don't Handle Multi-line Destructuring
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/extractors.py`, lines 145-155
**Patterns:**
```python
re.compile(
    r"(?:export\s+)?(?:const|let)\s+(\w+)"
    r"(?:\s*:\s*React\.FC\w*<[^>]*>)?"
    r"\s*=\s*\(\s*\{([^}]*)\}",
    re.DOTALL,
),
re.compile(
    r"(?:export\s+)?function\s+(\w+)\s*\(\s*\{([^}]*)\}",
    re.DOTALL,
),
```
**Problem:** With `re.DOTALL`, `[^}]*` will match across lines until the first `}` in the file content. For components with nested objects in their destructuring like `({ config: { a, b }, ...rest })`, the `[^}]*` stops at the inner `}`, capturing only `config: { a, b` -- corrupting the prop extraction.

Also, `[^>]*` in the first pattern stops at the first `>` in a generic type, which breaks on `React.FC<Props<T>>`.

**Severity:** LOW -- the prop extraction will be incomplete but won't crash. The passthrough detection just won't find as many passthrough props.

---

### Issue 20: `interface_re` Pattern Over-Matches
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/detectors/props.py`, line 19-22
**Pattern:**
```python
_BLOAT_SUFFIXES = r"(?:Props|Context|ContextValue|ContextType|State|StateValue)\w*"
interface_re = re.compile(
    rf"(?:export\s+)?(?:interface|type)\s+(\w+{_BLOAT_SUFFIXES})\s*(?:=\s*)?{{",
    re.MULTILINE,
)
```
**Problem:** The `\w+` before the suffix group is greedy and will consume the suffix itself if possible. For example, matching `interface FooProps` -- `\w+` would try to match `FooProps` as the non-suffix part, then `(?:Props|...)` would need to match the empty string (which it can't since `Props` requires 5 chars). Actually, `\w+` is greedy so it matches as much as possible first, then backtracks. The capture group `(\w+{_BLOAT_SUFFIXES})` captures the FULL name including the suffix, which is correct behavior.

Actually, I re-read this -- the capture group `(\w+(?:Props|Context|...)...)` means `\w+` can match zero-length after backtracking? No, `\w+` requires at least one character. So `FooProps` -- `\w+` tries all of `FooProps`, then `(?:Props...)` needs to match empty. Backtracks: `\w+` matches `FooProp`, `(?:Props...)` needs to match `s`. No. Backtracks to `Foo`, `(?:Props...)` matches `Props`. OK, this is correct.

The actual issue: `\w*` at the end of the suffix group means it will match `PropsExtended`, `ContextManager`, etc. -- names that happen to contain the suffix but aren't prop interfaces. This is intentional (matching things like `PropsWithChildren`), so it's by design.

**Severity:** LOW -- working as intended, just somewhat broad.

---

### Issue 21: `_EMPTY_CALLBACK_RE` Pattern May Miss Multi-line Callbacks
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/fixers/logs.py`, lines 170-180
**Pattern:**
```python
_EMPTY_CALLBACK_RE = re.compile(
    r"""
    ^\s*
    (?:
        \.then\s*\(\s*\(?[^)]*\)?\s*=>\s*\{\s*\}\s*\)
        | \.catch\s*\(\s*\(?[^)]*\)?\s*=>\s*\{\s*\}\s*\)
    )
    \s*[;,]?\s*$
    """,
    re.VERBOSE,
)
```
**Problem:** `[^)]*` inside `\(?[^)]*\)?` will greedily consume until the first `)`. For a callback with typed params like `.catch((err: Error) => {})`, the `[^)]*` matches `err: Error`, which is correct. But for `.catch(({code, message}: ApiError) => {})`, `[^)]*` would stop at the `)` inside `})`, matching incorrectly.

**Severity:** LOW -- edge case; destructured callback params are uncommon in `.catch()` and `.then()`.

---

### Issue 22: Missing `re.MULTILINE` for `^` Anchored Patterns in SMELL_CHECKS
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/detectors/smells.py`, lines 36, 128
**Patterns:** `r"^\s*//\s*@ts-nocheck"` and `r"^\s*void\s+[a-zA-Z_]\w*\s*;?\s*$"`
**Problem:** These patterns use `^` and `$` anchors. They are applied via `re.search(check["pattern"], line)` where `line` is a single line from `content.splitlines()`. Since each `line` is a single line (no embedded newlines), `^` and `$` match line boundaries correctly without `re.MULTILINE`. So this is NOT actually a bug -- it works because the patterns are applied line-by-line.

**Severity:** NONE -- false alarm, but worth noting that the anchors are redundant when using `re.search` on single-line inputs (they would be necessary only for multi-line string inputs).

---

### Issue 23: `supabase\.\w+\.\w+\.\w+` Too Rigid
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/detectors/concerns.py`, line 41
**Pattern:** `r"supabase\.\w+\.\w+\.\w+"`
**Problem:** This requires exactly 3 `.` accesses after `supabase`, which would match `supabase.from.select.eq` but not `supabase.from("table")` (since `\w+` won't match `(`). Also won't match the common pattern `supabase.from("users").select("*")` because the string arguments break the chain.

**Severity:** LOW -- this is a heuristic concern detector; false negatives reduce sensitivity but don't cause incorrect results.

---

### Issue 24: `_EXPORT_GREP_PAT` and `EXPORT_DECL_RE` Inconsistency
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/detectors/exports.py`, lines 13-18
**Patterns:**
```python
EXPORT_DECL_RE = re.compile(
    r"^export\s+(?:declare\s+)?(?:const|let|function|class|type|interface|enum)\s+(\w+)",
    re.MULTILINE,
)
_EXPORT_GREP_PAT = r"^export\s+(declare\s+)?(const|let|function|class|type|interface|enum)\s+\w+"
```
**Problem:** `EXPORT_DECL_RE` includes a capture group for the name `(\w+)` and makes `declare` non-capturing. `_EXPORT_GREP_PAT` captures both `declare` and the keyword but NOT the name. These two patterns are used for different purposes (grep vs precise extraction), which is fine, but the grep pattern captures `declare` and the keyword separately, which is never used.

More importantly, neither pattern handles `export async function` or `export default class` or `export abstract class`. The `EXPORT_DECL_RE` has `declare` as optional but not `async` or `default` or `abstract`.

**Severity:** LOW -- `async function` exports and `default` exports would be missed by dead export detection, but these are less common as dead exports.

---

### Issue 25: `_DEBUG_COMMENT_RE` Case-Insensitive and Broad
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/fixers/logs.py`, line 80-82
**Pattern:** `re.compile(r"(?:DEBUG|TEMP|LOG|TRACE|TODO\s*.*debug|HACK\s*.*log)", re.IGNORECASE)`
**Problem:** With `re.IGNORECASE`, this matches any comment containing "log", "temp", "debug", "trace" in any case. The `TODO\s*.*debug` part has `.*` which greedily matches everything between TODO and debug on the line. This could match legitimate comments like `// TODO: improve debugging experience` and incorrectly mark them as orphaned debug comments, leading to their deletion during log cleanup.

**Severity:** MEDIUM -- this is used in a fixer that modifies source code. False positives cause non-debug comments to be deleted.

---

### Issue 26: `\bfor\b` Pattern in String Context
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/__init__.py`, line 43
**Pattern:** `r"\[.*\bfor\b.*\bfor\b.*\]|\{.*\bfor\b.*\bfor\b.*\}"`
**Problem:** Beyond the backtracking issue (Issue 3), this pattern would match `for` inside string literals within list comprehensions: `[f"waiting for {x} for {y}" for x in items]` -- the two `for` keywords in the f-string would trigger a false positive.

**Severity:** LOW -- used as a complexity heuristic, so false positives just slightly inflate complexity scores.

---

### Issue 27: `debug_tag` Pattern Inconsistency Between Python and TypeScript
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/detectors/smells.py`, line 32
**Pattern:** `r"""(?:f?['"])\[([A-Z][A-Z0-9_]{2,})\]\s"""`
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/detectors/smells.py`, line 108
**Pattern:** `r"""(?:['"`])\[([A-Z][A-Z0-9_]{2,})\]\s"""`

**Problem:** The Python pattern specifically handles `f'` prefix but not `r'`, `b'`, or `rf'`. The TS pattern handles backtick but the Python one doesn't (not needed for Python). Minor inconsistency: the Python pattern requires `f?` prefix explicitly, meaning bare-string tags like `"[DEBUG] message"` would still match (since `f?` is optional). So both work similarly, just with different quote types. This is acceptable language-specific behavior.

**Severity:** LOW -- the patterns are appropriate for their respective languages.

---

### Issue 28: `workaround_tag` Pattern Quadratic Risk
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/detectors/smells.py`, line 140
**Pattern:** `r"//.*\[([A-Z][a-z]+(?:[A-Z][a-z]+)+)\]"`
**Problem:** The `.*` after `//` greedily consumes the entire line, then backtracks to find `[`. The PascalCase pattern `[A-Z][a-z]+(?:[A-Z][a-z]+)+` requires at least two camelCase segments. On a long comment line with a `[` near the end but no matching PascalCase tag, the `(?:[A-Z][a-z]+)+` group will try every partition before failing. This is O(n) backtracking (linear, not catastrophic) because each `[A-Z][a-z]+` alternative must start with uppercase, limiting partition points.

**Severity:** LOW -- not catastrophic, just slightly inefficient.

## RECOMMENDATIONS (Priority Order)

1. **Fix Issue 1 immediately** -- the TS function extractor regex can cause hangs on adversarial input. Restructure to avoid the `([^)]*\)|[^=])*` nested quantifier.

2. **Fix Issues 4 and 5** -- the `[^)]*` with DOTALL for Python function signatures is incorrect for any signature with nested parentheses (type annotations, default values with function calls). Replace with proper paren-depth tracking.

3. **Fix Issue 8** -- add string/comment awareness to the `catch_re.finditer(content)` calls, or pre-strip strings/comments from the content before searching.

4. **Fix Issue 9** -- the comment-stripping regex `re.sub(r"//[^\n]*", ...)` is applied to code bodies that may contain string literals with `//`. Use a string-aware comment stripper (the codebase already has `_build_ts_line_state` which could be adapted).

5. **Fix Issue 14** -- add `\(` to the except clause pattern to handle tuple exception handling.

6. **Resolve Issue 13** -- the _smell_helpers.py file is a complete duplicate of the multi-line detectors in smells.py. Only one copy should exist.

7. **Fix Issue 3** -- replace `.*` with `.*?` or bounded character classes in the nested comprehension pattern.

8. **Fix Issue 25** -- the `_DEBUG_COMMENT_RE` is too broad and could cause legitimate comments to be deleted during log cleanup.</result>

   - String-aware comment stripping (`_strip_ts_comments` vs naive `re.sub`)
   - Paren-depth tracking as replacement for `[^)]*` regex with DOTALL (fails on nested parens like type annotations)
   - XSS prevention in HTML visualization (esc() helper, `</` escaping in JSON)
   - Atomic file writes (temp + os.replace pattern)
   - Error handling patterns for file I/O and subprocess calls
   - `_json_default` for consistent JSON serialization (handles sets, Paths)
   - Scoring dimension system — detectors mapped to dimensions, with tier weights
   - DETECTOR_TOOLS in narrative.py — maps detectors to fixers and guidance

     - Added to SMELL_CHECKS: unreachable_code (high), constant_return (medium), regex_backtrack (high), naive_comment_strip (medium)
     - Added to `_detect_ast_smells` dispatch: `_detect_unreachable_code`, `_detect_constant_return`, `_detect_regex_backtrack`, `_detect_naive_comment_strip`
     - Key implementation - `_detect_unreachable_code`:
       ```python
       def _detect_unreachable_code(filepath: str, tree: ast.Module, smell_counts: dict[str, list]):
           _TERMINAL = (ast.Return, ast.Raise, ast.Break, ast.Continue)
           def _check_block(stmts: list[ast.stmt]):
               for i, stmt in enumerate(stmts):
                   if isinstance(stmt, _TERMINAL) and i < len(stmts) - 1:
                       next_stmt = stmts[i + 1]
                       if isinstance(next_stmt, ast.Expr) and isinstance(next_stmt.value, ast.Constant):
                           continue
                       smell_counts["unreachable_code"].append({...})
                   for attr in ("body", "orelse", "finalbody", "handlers"):
                       block = getattr(stmt, attr, None)
                       if isinstance(block, list):
                           child_stmts = [s for s in block if isinstance(s, ast.stmt)]
                           if child_stmts:
                               _check_block(child_stmts)
       ```
     - Key implementation - `_detect_constant_return`: Skips None returns (procedures), requires >=2 returns + conditional logic, >=4 LOC, no decorators
     - Key implementation - `_detect_regex_backtrack`: Only flags `+`/`*` outer quantifiers (not `?`), filters negated char classes `[^X]*` and literal-anchored `\.\w+`
       ```python
       _NESTED_QUANT = re.compile(
           r"\([^)]*[+*][^)]*\)[+*]"
           r"|"
           r"\(\?:[^)]*[+*][^)]*\)[+*]"
       )
       ```
     - Key implementation - `_detect_naive_comment_strip`: Flags `re.sub` calls with `//`, `/*`, `#` patterns where replacement is empty

       ```python
       for line in lines:
           for m in provider_open.finditer(line):
               depth += 1
               provider_stack.append(m.group(1))
               if depth > max_depth:
                   max_depth = depth
                   providers_at_max = list(provider_stack)
           for m in provider_close.finditer(line):
               if provider_stack and provider_stack[-1] == m.group(1):
                   provider_stack.pop()
                   depth -= 1
       ```

       ```python
       pre_depth = depth
       # ... process braces on line ...
       ```

     ```python
     def compute_max_params(content: str, lines: list[str]) -> tuple[int, str] | None:
         def_re = re.compile(r"def\s+\w+\s*\(", re.MULTILINE)
         max_params = 0
         for m in def_re.finditer(content):
             depth = 1
             i = m.end()
             while i < len(content) and depth > 0:
                 ch = content[i]
                 if ch == "(": depth += 1
                 elif ch == ")": depth -= 1
                 i += 1
             if depth != 0: continue
             param_str = content[m.end():i - 1]
             params = [p.strip() for p in param_str.split(",") if p.strip()]
             real_params = [p for p in params if p not in ("self", "cls") and not p.startswith("*")]
             if len(real_params) > max_params:
                 max_params = len(real_params)
         if max_params > 7:
             return max_params, f"function with {max_params} params"
         return None
     ```

Fixed by removing `?` from the outer quantifier pattern — only `+` and `*` can cause ReDoS. Also needed to filter safe patterns: negated char classes and literal-anchored subpatterns.

Here is the comprehensive Python style review of the desloppify codebase.
